Summary

Title may change, it's a working title.

Kryptonians have a soulmate, and Kara was sure hers died when Krypton exploded. Her mark has stayed dormant for years, and she accepted that it likely would never activate.

But then she meets Lena Luthor.

Lena has enough to worry about with renaming her company and making up for what Lex did in Metropolis. National City is supposed to be a fresh start to really turn her family name into something good. She doesn't have time for friends, let alone romance.

But then Kara Danvers walks into her life.

Notes

This is not one of the fics I asked about before posting Paws, I know. But I was inspired to write this first scene with the sex, and then was inspired to make it a soulmate fic and... well... this happened.

Enjoy?

    I am not very confident in this chapter, to the point I nearly deleted the whole thing and restarted. But in the end, I decided I liked most of it and didn't have anything better in mind for how this interaction would go, so I'm posting it.

She'd done a little looking into the woman after she'd come in with Clark Kent the other day and couldn't deny she was impressed by what she'd seen. Even if she ignored the Instagram photos, all of which seemed to include food or animals, the fact that Kara Danvers was a secretarial legend for surviving working under Cat Grant made her extremely interesting. Given she hadn't published anything yet, Lena wasn't sure how to handle the woman as a reporter, but she hoped that the blonde would give her a little benefit of the doubt after the way she seemed to sympathize with what Lena was doing with L-Corp by trying to separate from the Luthor name.

    Chapter End Notes

    If you don't want to hear about my mental health, you can move on. The only thing you really need to know is that I may have some delays on writing and that I'm really thankful for all of the kudos and comments I get from you guys.

    But if you want to know the longer version...

    I'm an anxious, depressed mess. I know, lots of people have that issue. But I still have to live with my mess and I struggle. I've been on meds for my issues for... I dunno, over a decade? And I usually hate being on anti-depressants because they put my brain in a fog and I can't write. I've been on a low dose of my current one for a while and it's been fine, but recently I've been having a lot more trouble with suicidal ideation and so I decided to increase my dose.

    I'm going to work hard to keep my writing going still. It helps that you guys are so amazing and your comments inspire me to keep going. But for the next month, I may have even more trouble than usual if the increased dose puts my brain in a fog.

    You may wonder then why I have a month between chapters at times if this is the first med change in a while. I actually have like... 8 Supercorp fics, 6 Mass Effect fics, and 5 novels that all are a work in progress just sitting there. I will sometimes have an idea for one and that's the one I write on for a while even if it's not the one I'm working on for posting here.

    I don't write a whole story and then post, usually I get a few chapters max and then I'm too impatient to share it. I don't like having more than one fic active at a time so I try to focus more on the one I'm currently posting, but sometimes I just have a block and it takes time to work past it - kinda like this chapter.

    All this is to say, I may or may not have a chapter out soon if the increase in my dose fogs up my brain like I'm anxious it will. If I can still get some writing done with the higher dose, even if it's a bit harder, and be happier with my overall mental health... That's a win in my book. Sometimes you have to take those small victories when you can.

As she sank into the mattress, Kara reached out with her hearing to check on her soulmate and relaxed further when she heard the slow and steady beating of sleep.

    Lena sat at her desk and stared at the device that she'd removed from her safe to think about what she wanted to do. She told Kara she'd destroy it and the files, and she'd already shredded the other items she'd been handed by her board, but for some reason she was waffling over the alien detection device.

    "It's fine, darling." That... wasn't supposed to come out. Pet names were for dates not friends! Though, Kara didn't seem phased as she lopped their arms together and they walked towards the doorway leading towards the section of the building where the gala was taking place. "Even if you did get something on this, so long as you enjoy yourself it will be worth it."

     

     

    Kara scoffed, though the grin she had made it soft, and said, "I'm guaranteed to have fun, Lena. I'm getting to hang out with you!"

     

     

    Warmth blossomed in her chest, and her cheeks. "That's sweet of you, Kara, but I have no doubt that after this one you'll be loathe to join me at another one of these."

     

     

    "Please. I will always be happy to go to these things with you."

     

     

    Predictably, Kara led the way to where refreshments were spread out so she could sample the offerings. Lena made a note to look into using the catering company more often for her own events the more Kara seemed to approve of the things she was eating, offering Lena bites of her favorites with a shy smile like she did whenever they ate somewhere together. It made her wonder if there was something about the action that was important to the blonde's culture, but most attempts to learn what sort of alien Kara was had been rebuffed kindly so she was afraid to ask about that sort of thing.

     

     

    Eventually Lena was able to lead Kara away from the food and towards the bar, both getting something more to their taste than the champagne being offered by the handful of catering waiters meandering through those gathered. It had been a few days since they had seen each other so they caught up on recent events as they sipped their drinks and wandered the room. Occasionally Lena would be pulled into conversation or would have to take a moment to talk to people she needed for networking.

     

     

    Finally it felt like they'd completed the social games for the night, and both took a little break at one of the tables. They leaned against the small, circular surface covered by a white cloth, drinks in hand and another plate of food that Kara had carried over. They both nibbled at it, though Lena only when Kara wanted her to try something.

     

     

    "We have game nights at least once a month, though we try for more than that," Kara was telling her, the topic having moved to their friends. Lena had commented that she only knew a small handful of people in the city, and Kara explained she didn't have that many close friends. "Which you're welcome to come to if you'd like, by the way. I was going to invite you to the next one which was supposed to be tomorrow but the guys decided we should go out for a drink and Alex asked me to take them to Al's."

     

     

    Lena was aware enough from Kara's other stories who the guys were - James Olsen and Winn Schott - and that Alex was her sister, but she hasn't met them yet. Part of that was because she didn't want to share the blonde, but she was also really nervous. Would Kara still want to be around her if her friends didn't like her? 

     

     

    "They've never been to your bar?"

     

     

    Kara shook her head. "No. It's kinda been my place to be out, you know? Alex doesn't want anyone to know about my genealogy, and Winn didn't know until last year which is also when I met James. The only friend I have that's been there is Tenar."

     

     

    That wasn't a name Lena had heard before, which made her stomach knot up a little. Kara was extremely happy to talk about her friends and family, so to learn that there might be some close friends that she hadn't heard about yet made Lena wonder if they were more... more something. And Lena wanted to be the one who was more than anyone else. Which, now that she was thinking about it, was likely not a positive way to look at their friendship.

     

     

    Oh well. She'd request a video call from her therapist Sunday and talk about it, but there was no needn't worry about your extremely possessive view of Kara's attentions.

     

     

    "I don't think you've told me about Tenar," she said instead, trying to sound casual.

     

     

    "That's probably because I wasn't sure how to explain my friendship with her," Kara admitted. "It's not really something I want to share just yet either, though I do promise to talk about it soon."

     

     

    That was reasonable, Lena decided. She offered a grin and put a hand on Kara's forearm. "I understand. Take your time." They stood smiling at each other for a moment, and then Lena motioned towards the open floor and asked, "Dance?"

     

     

    "I'd love to."

     

     

    They had only just gotten into position and barely began moving when one of the walls near them exploded inward.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    I'm going to have another little mental health bit here, so if you don't want to read about it you can move on. There won't be anything else in this note. :)

    I decided that I didn't want to take any of my meds, which we were all reminded why that was bad when I nearly committed suicide on Saturday. I'm currently safe and we've got an action plan for if I get that bad again, but I was alone until just before I implemented my suicide plan so I didn't have help breaking the cycle until it was as bad as it was. Luckily some friends showed up before I did anything and pretty much saved my life. I've been mostly in the upswing since but my partner knows if I have another severe drop again that we will be calling the crisis center and taking me in.

    Chapter 5

    Chapter Notes

    I'm alive. I don't think I like this chapter very much at all. But it had the points I wanted it to have (sort of) and I wanted to post something. It's also a bit shorter than I wanted, but I'm hitting a wall so I figured I'd post this and let myself sit on it a bit. My depression isn't helping I'm sure.

    I am doing better. Not good, but better.

    Kara immediately moved between Lena and the wall, feeling the impact of some of the debris against her back. She saw a few others who were also near the explosion get hurt, and she was sure that several at the location may not have survived. Relying on her senses and the fact that her brain could parse through the input at ridiculous speeds, she was quick to learn several things: 

    There were six attackers, one female and all human.

    The wall that they blew a hole into was load bearing.

    She was not wearing her suit under this dress and was unlikely to get to her apartment and back before more people got hurt, or worse, even if she thought Lena would let her out of her sight.

     

     

    "This is definitely not how I wanted to tell you," Kara mumbled, making Lena look at her in confusion before green eyes took in the scene and her face turned hard. Kara took out her phone and dialed, making her friend look even more awkward. "Alex? Yeah, so, remember how we were making up contingencies the other night for if I ever did something really stupid that would expose my identity?"

     

     

    "Kara, what the hell is going on?" her sister growled.

     

     

    "Might wanna start implementing one. Oh, and send help." Then she hung up, ignoring her sister's yelling and handed the phone to Lena, moving as the attackers did to keep herself between them and the brunette. "If Alex calls back three times, answer for me? She probably will need your help to coordinate with security anyway."

     

     

    "Wha-"

     

     

    Bullets rained, though most of them were aimed at the ceiling as the criminals just wanted to scare the inhabitants. Folks were already trying to leave from the main doors, but the congestion meant it was slow and panicked. Kara let her body slide into superspeed and zipped around the group, blocking the bullets that were headed towards civilians and then taking the guns to hang them from the ceiling lights.

     

     

    That didn't stop the building from groaning loudly and beginning to shake a little, and with the guns taken care of Kara moved to the broken section floating and holding it up. She huffed once, then roared to displace some of the tension in her body to keep from causing more damage, and did her best to ignore the pointing and exclamations as people realized that she wasn't in her suit.

     

     

    Lena was gaping at her, not seeming to notice that the phone in her hand was ringing. The entire event to this moment took less than two minutes, and security was only just realizing that the attackers had no other methods of harm and were moving in quickly to apprehend the criminals, who were preparing to run as they realized the same thing.

     

     

    Kara continued to hold the building as it finished evacuating. It wasn't one of the skyscrapers, luckily, but it was still a twenty stories tall high class hotel. She wasn't really tired yet, but the strain on her body was starting to make itself known. She watched as Lena stayed close, her expression closed and guarded, and refused to leave when the security team tried to escort her out.

     

     

    Alex showed up after the room was emptied, though Kara knew they hadn't finished evacuating the building yet. The redhead stomped over and frowned, fists resting on her hips as she took in the sight.

     

     

    "Dammit, Kara," she grumbled, shaking her head and sighing. "I've got Henshaw working shit out, but this... Fuck, kid. Our tech team isn't gonna be able to shit this down."

     

     

    Kara huffed out a laugh. "We knew it was likely to happen someday, Alex. It's not like most of the alien population couldn't tell as soon as they were close to me who I was. One of them would have sold me out eventually."

     

     

    Alex scowled. "Still would've been better than this. We could have found a way to change the narrative somehow that way."

     

     

    "Eh. It'll be fine." Kara grunted as the building shifted and she had to make a change in her own stance. "Did you tell Eliza?"

     

     

    "Of course I did. She's probably going to chew me out for it later for sure." Alex groaned. "Why couldn't you have waited until after Thanksgiving?"

     

     

    "Sure, Alex. Next time I'll have the bad guys wait a couple weeks before they attack a building in such a way that I can't go get my suit unless I want to risk even more lives," the blonde snarked.

     

     

    "Yes, do that. Send out a memo or something."

     

     

    "Alex."

     

     

    Alex huffed and walked closer, eying Lena who hadn't left the area either. "You holding that up all right?"

     

     

    "Easier than catching a plane," Kara quipped, looking up into the building with her supervision. "Hey, let security know that there's a family trapped by debris on floor twelve? Some of the floor above it caved in before I could catch it, and they can't get out."

     

     

    Her sister spoke into an ear comm, relaying the information. Once a team was confirmed to be on its way, she turned her attention back to Kara - or rather, to Lena's attention on Kara. "You should probably evacuate as well, Miss Luthor."

     

     

    Lena eyed her for a moment before shaking her head once. "I'm not leaving."

     

     

    "You do realize she's going to worry about you being in here in case more of the building falls."

     

     

    "Are you leaving?" Alex narrowed her eyes and Lena smirked. "Then I don't know why you think you'll be able to convince me to leave." 

     

     

    "If you two aren't nice to each other, I'll call for someone to drag both of you out," Kara huffed. "It's distracting enough that you're still in the danger zone." And then she started humming that song, because it came up and was catchy.

     

     

    "Who the hell would you call?"

     

     

    Kara stopped humming the song to hum in contemplating. "I mean, I have like... a dozen people who would probably be willing to help me out."

     

     

    "Are they like Tenar?" Alex grumbled. "Aliens you've slept with?"

     

     

    Lena frowned at that information, but the sisters didn't notice as Kara exclaimed, "Not all of them! A few of them don't have the ability to do that sort of thing with our body type. Like, Kip is basically a asexual, like amoebas. He doesn't need another for procreation, it's only possible once a decade, and he doesn't have sexual erotic zones, and he takes no pleasure in being intimate with someone who does have them."

     

     

    "And can Kip help you hold up a building or physically remove two human women from this building?" Alex asked.

     

     

    Kara frowned. "No. He's too small for that. His species rarely gets taller than four feet, and if he's more than fifty pounds then I'd be surprised."

     

     

    Lena finally seemed to realize that Alex was trying to distract Kara from the weight of the building, making her less likely to lose her grip on it. "And how many aliens have you slept with?"

     

     

    That question, specifically because it was asked by her soulmate, made Kara's face flush from embarrassment. "Um. Maybe twenty? I didn't have a problem with sleeping with someone again, and it was all consensual and only about physical pleasure, so-"

     

     

    "Oh god, I don't need to know this," Alex moaned, rubbing her forehead. "I can't even cover my ears because I need to be focused on relaying info about the evacuation."

     

     

    "You could leave your comm device with me and leave," Lena drawled, smirking at the redhead. "I could relay information to Kara as needed."

     

     

    "Like hell. I don't trust you," Alex snarled.

     

     

    "Alex!"

     

     

    "I'm not going to lie about it, Kara!"

     

     

    "You promised you wouldn't judge her."

     

     

    "And I don't trust her on principle because i don't know her," Alex pointed out. "I didn't trust Tenar either when I first met her."

     

     

    Kara tilted her head a little as she considered, and then nodded once. "Okay, fair. But you can still be nicer about it." There was some huffing but Alex didn't deny that. She checked the building again with her vision, and the family from floor 12 was being escorted down the stairs. She didn't see anyone else, at least living, anywhere else. "The floor 12 civilians are being escorted down the stairs. No one else is in the rest of the building."

     

     

    Alex relayed that to the crew outside and then turned to Kara. "What will you need once the building is evacuated?"

     

     

    Kara hummed as she considered that and looked around. "Well, we just need to shore up this wall since it was load bearing. I know a couple Branx - and no, I didnt sleep with them... both," Alex made a choking noise while Lena looked a little confused and something a little darker, "who would be willing to carry in some steel beams if we could get some here."

     

     

    "I can make a call to get the steel here," Lena told them, holding up her cell phone. "How do we contact these Branx?"

     

     

    "Call Al's. They're usually there about now. Tell M'gann I need U'varr and Ig'narr. Alex, let the crews know that two big four-armed brutes will be coming to help me."

     

     

    "Four arms?" Alex balked. "Is that why you..?"

     

     

    "Well, they're also really hardy. Great choice for angry sex before I had a good handle on my powers. I don't have quite the problem with control anymore regardless of my mood so it's been a while. They're great drinking buddies now."

     

     

    Lena was a little terse when she spoke to M'gann, but the bartender was quick to call for the two while still on the line and explained what Kara needed. A moment later, M'gann relayed that they had run outside to their bikes and would be there soon. Once off the phone, she relayed that information to Alex who told her teams.

     

     

    "This playing relay telephone shit is annoying," the redhead grumbled. 

     

     

    It took almost three hours before Kara was able to release her hold on the building. She was exhausted and wobbled a little when her feet touched the ground, causing one of the two Branx to catch her with two of his arms until she felt steady enough to let go.

     

     

    "Thanks, Ig'narr. Hey, darts on Friday?"

     

     

    "Yes. I have need to defeat you," the bulky alien told her with a wide grin.

     

     

    "Psh. Good luck," U'varr commented, chuckling. "Kara has good hand-eye coordination."

     

     

    "You know all about her hands."

     

     

    "Whoa!" Kara interjected, laughing. "Let's not go there today, fellas. My sister is here!" She motioned towards Alex and Lena, and the two aliens turned.

     

     

    "Which?" U'varr asked, eyes taking both in.

     

     

    Alex raised a hand, frowning at the trio. "That would be me."

     

     

    "I would rather not hear about such things either," Lena drawled, not meeting Kara's eyes. She started typing away at her phone to busy herself from the temptation.

     

     

    The Branx duo exchanged looks, and Ig'narr mumbled, "You are familiar to me."

     

     

    "She's Lena Luthor," U'varr grumbled, crossing his lower arms. "Are you to make sure we do not evil?"

     

     

    Lena's head shot up and Kara quickly moved to face the both of them, Lena at her back. "She's my shesur:zhaol and nothing like her family. I trust her with my life."

     

     

    Ig'narr nodded. "Understand. You have instincts. We trust." U'varr nodded his agreement. "We finished now, so home go. Yes?"

     

     

    Kara nodded. "Yeah, head on home. I'll see you at Al's on Friday for darts." They ambled away, and Kara turned to her sister and soulmate with a small grin. "That was fun. I'm beat, and starving."

     

     

    "You can't go home," Alex announced with a sigh. "The place is a zoo with reporters waiting for you to come home. My place isn't safe either."

     

     

    "Mine will be," Lena interjected, her phone put away even though she still hadn't really looked at Kara. "I have my own security system in place, and it's not under my name so no one knows that I live there."

     

     

    "Who's name is it under?"

     

     

    "A pseudonym. I use it for some things, and I'm not comfortable telling a black ops agent what it is."

     

     

    Alex nodded. "Fair enough. It'll be safe for you both? And this is an invitation for my sister to stay?"

     

     

    "Yes, to both questions."

     

     

    "Good enough for me." Alex turned to Kara. "I'll try to get some stuff for you tomorrow, but you won't have access to clothes for tonight. I've got a duffel with some of my workout stuff at base if you wanna fly out there before going to Lena's."

     

     

    Kara shook her head. "There is no way I have the energy to fly out there tonight. If the sun was out, I might make it there and back, but I feel way too drained tonight."

     

     

    "I have some things you can wear tonight," Lena told her, finally looking up at Kara. "My driver can't get through, so we'd have to walk through the chaos outside to get to him."

     

     

    "That's doable," Kara said with a smile and a nod.

     

    Chapter 6

    Chapter Notes

    This is short. It was a good end point for the chapter though and I'm ready to move to the next thing with the story so... yeah.

    Enjoy?

    Lena was quiet most of the way to her home. Part of the reason why was the burning in her chest as she learned about Kara's previous partners and how friendly she was with them. She wasn't sure how she could compete with the hardy Branx with four arms, and she was sure there were others who were more... something. She knew she was attractive, but she was only human and Kara was Supergirl.

     

    Which was another reason she was quiet. Her mind was whirring with the few times she'd seen Supergirl up close. Really, as soon as Kara had shown her the alien detection device with the red light, she should have been able to put the clues together. But she hadn't wanted to dig deeper than Kara was willing to show, had wanted to earn her trust, and so she had missed obvious clues.

     

    The words Kara told the Branx just a short while ago came to mind and she let her lips curl into a small smile. She wasn't sure what that musical word in what was likely Kryptonian was, but Kara had said she trusted her with her life. That pushed the burning aside a little, letting her feel the joy of having that much of Kara's trust.

     

    "Would you like to go with me to the bar next Friday?" Kara suddenly asked, filling the car with her voice and pulling Lena with her thoughts. "You've been there now, and it's one of my favorite places. Alex is going to go, and she's never been there. I think she's curious about it. I plan to drink something that actually gets me drunk a little. I won't drink a lot because I can't lose control of myself too much, but it's fun to let loose a bit. My friend Tenar will likely be there too. She's nice and wants to meet you as well."

     

    Lena smiled as Kara rambled, but eventually reached out to put a hand on Kara's arm in a light touch. "If you're all right with me crashing your party, I would love to come."

     

    Kara grinned. "You wouldn't be crashing if you're invited."

     

    The name Tenar was familiar, but Lena pushed that away for now. She felt too happy with the invitation to consider if that was one of the people Kara had slept with. "Then I would love to join you."

     

    The car pulled onto a long drive hidden by thick tree cover that Lena had specifically had added when she'd purchased the place to keep the house hidden from the street. There was a gate up a ways, also hidden from the street by the foliage, and a fence that surrounded the rest of her land here. Her driver took a moment to use his phone to enter a passcode before the gates opened, and once it was he cruised through. The drive still meandered a bit longer, but the house was in view shortly.

     

    It was a large building, sprawling but only two stories high. She was sure it would be classed as a mansion from square footage, but the aesthetic was more farmhouse with warm and inviting colors on the outside. She didn't have a barn, though there was another building several hundred feet back away from the house. The attached garage had three separate doors with plenty of space between them, looking more like a five car garage that had been remodeled to have only three single car doors. The house itself was a light amber color with dark green shutters, door, and metal roof. A wide wraparound porch had the railing painted that same shade of green while the porch itself was ebony colored wood.

     

    "It's beautiful," Kara murmured as she looked over the building in awe. "I thought you had a penthouse near L-Corp though?"

     

    Lena nodded as the car pulled to a stop and explained as they got out to head inside. "I do. It's my public address, and I do tend to stay there more often especially during the week depending on what's going on at work. But this fits your needs better, or rather Alex's. Frank can only come and go with a passcode I give him that day and I have a housekeeper with her own access code that only works on Wednesdays when she comes in and changes monthly, but no one else other than myself and Jess can access the property any time without flight or risking themselves on my fence which has alerts set up so I will be informed as well as a private security company and the police."

     

    "How do you get takeout delivered here?" Kara asked, sounding perturbed by the idea that that wouldn't be an option.

     

    "I rarely have it delivered here," Lena told her. "I have a chef's kitchen and enjoy cooking to a degree, so I haven't had a need to often. If I'm too tired to cook and I'm coming out here, I typically get food on my way."

     

    Kara nodded, still seeming a bit upset at the news. Then Lena realized it must be because the blonde needed to eat a lot and would feel uncomfortable asking Lena to cook for her. She was likely starving right now as well with as much energy as she'd had to expend at the hotel.

     

    "Why don't we go cook something up now? I'm a bit hungry myself. I could use a hand with some cutting and other prep as well to make it go faster," she said, hoping that the wording would help with the blonde's pride and make it easier to accept the meal.

     

    She shouldn't have been too concerned, though, because Kara had perked up at the mention of food and was nodding before she'd even finished her statement. "Gosh, I would gladly help. I am starving!"

     

    They moved through the open foyer and living room space to a gorgeous and spacious kitchen after going through a set of doors. There was a smaller dining set in the room tucked away, and through another door Kara could see a formal dining room that looked rarely used. But most of her attention was on Lena as the brunette moves through the kitchen with ease and familiarity, opening a door and disappearing into a large pantry. Kara peeked in and frowned at how little was in it, mostly dry and canned goods that were shelf stable for long periods as well as a handful of perishables leaving more than two-thirds of the space free. But then she remembered that Lena wasn't here that often and decided that maybe this was enough for a single woman for the few times she was able to cook here.

     

    Lena was efficient and patient as Kara worked on food prep, turning to cook things whenever she could once Kara was instructed on what she needed to do. Cutting up everything was easy, and Kara hadn't bothered to hold back her abilities now that her soulmate was in the know about them, much to Lena's amusement. Seeing the knife blur as Kara sliced and diced the various vegetables that Lena had given her was a new experience for her, and she decided that it wasn't a bad one. It sped up food prep, for one, and seeing proof of Kara's ease around her was heartening.

     

    They chatted away as they worked, Kara complaining about her supervisor ("He's not a bad man, I'm sure, but would it hurt to be a little nicer?") while Lena worked through one of her personal projects aloud that she was having some trouble with after some encouragement from Kara that she didn't mind. As they waited for the casserole she'd made to finish up in the oven, Kara asked for some paper and proceeded to draw out a few things as well as write a few equations that Lena was able to work off of.

     

    "I'm surprised you know this," she commented as she started to continue with her work, amazed by the layers Kara seemed to have.

     

    The blonde shrugged. "That's actually pretty simple. I learned that stuff as a kid. I was the youngest member of the Science Guild when I left my planet," she explained. "I had learned Calculus by age four. Science and math came easy to me."

     

    "Is there anything you can't do?" Lena joked.

     

    Kara smiled warmly before admitting, "I can't bake."

     

    That had Lena laughing for a moment as she shook her head at the blonde. Then she asked, "Why be a reporter if you know so much about science?"

     

    The blonde shrugged and looked away, taking a moment to really consider her answer. "There's a few reasons for it, if I'm honest. The reason I usually tell people is that I have to hide how much I know so that it's harder to find out I'm an alien. And while that's true, I'm also intelligent enough to know how to downplay that so I could be in a scientific field if I wanted to. I almost went to school for environmental engineering, you know? It's what my specialty was going to be on Krypton, and it still would have presented some challenges for me considering the differences between the two planets.

     

    "But Alex was always the one who wanted to go into science coursework, and she'd had me foisted onto her when she didn't want a sibling. And then I ended up taking so much of her parents' attention, and then it was my fault Jeremiah was gone... I didn't want to take anything else from her. So I went for marketing instead, with a minor is English literature. Then the only job I managed to find when I moved here to be close to Alex was to be Cat Grant's assistant, though I did spend a little time working for Nooman's while sleeping on Alex's couch back when she rented the apartment I live in now. When I got the job at CatCo, she found her current place and moved out once she was sure I could afford to stay on my own.

     

    "But even then, there's the fact that any science like what you do, with inventions and stuff, just reminds me of my ukr, my father. He and I would spend hours together in his lab working together, and I just didn't like the idea of working in a lab without him." Kara smiled sadly,and Lena took hold of her hand and gave it a squeeze to show support. "I could probably handle it now, but I'm not sure. I've been on Earth longer than I'd been on Krypton, and it's been a recent change so I'm back to being just as sensitive about it as I'd been when I first landed here."

     

    "Well," Lena started hesitantly, "if you ever want to spend time in a lab with no pressure, you're always welcome to come by here." She pointed at the building off in the distance that Kara had seen when they pulled up and that was visible through the window. "That's my personal lab, and if you like we can go out there once we've eaten or even in the morning and I'll key you into the security system so you can have access to it anytime. I'll also give you the permanent access code in case you don't fly out here."

     

    That surprised Kara, though she was also a bit giddy that Lena seemed to trust her that much. "Are you sure you want to give me so much access to your home, Lena?"

     

    The brunette shrugged and offered a warm smile. "I'm very sure. You deserve to have access to a lab, Kara, if you want to. I can tell you really enjoy journalism, so I'm not about to try and convince you to leave it for the sciences. But if you want to, shouldn't you be able to use your knowledge?"

     

    Kara considered that before nodding slowly, looking out at the lab. "And if I make something that would benefit humans, would you try to profit from it?"

     

    "I can't deny that I'd probably want to market it," Lena admitted, "but I'd also talk to you about it to find out how you would want me to handle it. I could also create a small company under our perview that your work could come out of, perhaps marketed by L-Corp but our involvement could be determined by a contract that way."

     

    "That sounds agreeable, should I do anything," Kara told her. "I don't know how I'll feel about working in a lab without my father, but I do miss it."

     

    They moved the conversation to lighter things, continuing even as they ate. Lena had only a little, encouraging Kara to eat the rest if she was hungry and smiking fondly as the blonde finished up everything else. She made a mental note to have the house stocked up for the blonde, wanting to help her feel as welcome as possible in her space. It surprised her just how much she wanted Kara to be here, to be in her space and invading her life completely, but as she watched Kara eat Lena decided that this blonde alien was worth that and so much more.

    Chapter End Notes

    I'm going to share some updates about my mental health, so if you don't want to read about it, you can move on. I won't be offended!

    I'm doing better. Not great, not yet, but I think I can avoid going to the crisis center for now. I've been reading books rather than fanfic, which means that I haven't been as invested in writing (sorry) but I'm slowly putting my mind back to pen on paper (figuratively) and writing bits at a time again. I don't always work on this fic, as I have like a dozen other things I'm writing (several are books rather than fanfic) and I let inspiration guide me rather than desire for putting content out there.

    Because I was medication adverse for a while, I not only didn't take my mental health meds but also the meds for my back and legs and migraines. I've not cared that I was in pain either, because for a while my life wasn't worth the effort of taking care of it. I'm now at a point where I hurt while the meds do their job and I don't like but I want to be better again so I can do things I didn't want to do while depressed (like photography - I miss going on walks with my camera, and that's a new feeling since my depression started its decline).

    Basically, my mind and body are healing now. It's a process, but at least there's progress. I can't promise more frequent updates (not that I follow a posting schedule as is) but I do promise not to leave a fic unfinished since that annoys me if other writers do that.

    Thank you so much to all the support I've gotten in comments and such. You guys are amazing and I love you. It's helped so much to see the words you've shared, whether it's been about your own journeys with mental health, sending your support and care, or just focusing on the story with how you feel about it. I've loved every one of them and they have helped encourage me to write and to keep trying my best to get through my current wave of severe depression. Thank you so much, to all of you.

    Chapter 7

    Chapter Notes

    Another short-ish one. Hopefully you enjoy!

    Lena had spent most evenings at her home rather than her penthouse the next couple days while Kara was there. On Monday, Kara was gone before she woke and when she got to work there was a note on her computer in the blonde's handwriting telling her to watch the newsroom from CatCo at 8 - ("cool balcony btw, is that new?") She looked over at her balcony doors and considered them a moment before smiling softly and going to open them, glad that yhe contractors had been swift with her request for the rush order she'd sent in for it to be added.

     

     

    The 8 am broadcast included an interview with "Kara Zor-El Danvers, Supergirl" that had her speaking of her arrival and a little of her journey, though she refused to explain more than that she'd been delayed in a timeless void for over twenty years. She wouldn't expose who Superman was, chastising the Queen of Media fondly when she asked. The segment wasn't really all that long, but it humanized the alien and gave her a platform to do the same for the less human-looking aliens as well that she took advantage of.

     

     

    The increase in suggestions from her board for anti-alien tech after the interview made her frown, so she took great pleasure in turning down any of the more violent ideas shot her way but any that could be used for medicine, human or alien, she decided to tinker with at home.

     

     

    The rest of the week had little interaction between the two outside of texts, Lena swamped with PR stints and paperwork while Kara was stuck dealing with Cat Grant and the ramifications of the reveal of Supergirl's identity. There was an influx of national anti-alien sentiment, but for the most part National City seemed to weather it well for the week with more positive and less violent protests. Supergirl made her usual showings, nothing about what she did in the city changed by her reveal. CatCo had to handle several events, mostly those same protests as a couple days during the week being outside their building.

By Friday, she had mostly taken care of that with her PR team and an interview with a CatCo reporter where she admitted that she and Kara were friends, having met and talked during the blonde's interview of her and then continued communication afterwards. The fact that her friend was an alien, and specifically Supergirl, didn't change that relationship.

Knowing that she was about to meet another of Kara's sexual partners and the knowledge they were friends had her unsure how to handle the incoming social event. Her attraction had her feeling jealous, but logic told her that she had no right to be and she did her best to push it down. She didn't want Kara to notice and then not want her around anymore, to lose this friendship she was so glad to have and would never want to risk.

The table that she was aware was considered Kara's had two women sitting at it, one she recognized from the gala and the other completely unknown, though Lena could tell from a glance that she wasn't human. Even if lavender skin wasn't a dead giveaway, the addition of slightly pointed ears reminiscent of elves in the movies and slitted pupils like a cat was further proof of the woman's alienness.

There's more involved in that ability, but that's the easiest description. And Tenar, this is my shesur:zhaol, Lena Luthor."

She wasn't sure how she could handle being face to face with a woman thar Kara had apparently slept with a lot, and that apparently was not over it.

    "I'll just nibble on Kara's plates, I think," she said eventually, with the blonde's brightening expression telling her she said the right thing. "I'm not sure I'd eat much anyway, and given the variety she usually has it means I don't have to think about making anymore decisions after the kind of day I usually have."

Grumbling, she ordered a burger and fries before sulking a little. In contrast, Tenar seemed to approve of Lena's choice as she ordered something alien.

     

     

    "I'll get all that right in," M'gann told them before walking off.

     

     

    Kara immediately started asking about Lena's day, and she gladly talked about the meetings she'd been subjected to as well as the handful of proposals she'd had to review. "I'm getting really frustrated with the increased number of completely anti-alien tech I've had come across my desk just because people think that as a Luthor they'd have a better chance getting my company to fund and develop it."

     

     

    "You can't really be surprised, can you?" Tenar pointed out. "You haven't released any real statements with any pro-alien tech. Yeah, your PR has been denouncing the anti-alien sentiment, but you haven't really made your stance known. I mean, you admitted your friendship with Kara, who is now publicly known as Supergirl, and given the history between Superman and your brother that tells the more intelligent that you're not of the same mind as him, but the people who don't make that connection? All they see is that you aren't publicly anti-alien."

I suppose I've been so focused on denying my connections to Lex that I haven't really made my own stance known." She looked down, deep in thought. "But, honestly I'm not really sure what my stance is. Obviously I don't care that Kara is alien, but she's really the only one I'm familiar with. My other brief encounters with any non-humans have had her as a buffer or they have been wanting to attack me because of Lex."

     

     

    The thigh under her hand relaxed a little, and Lena turned to the blonde as she spoke. "What can I do to help you?"

     

     

    "I suppose I need to do some research into some things, but realistically... I need more exposure. And I should look into L-Corp's hiring policies, as well as its outreach programs. I need to make sure that the discrimination policies don't allow for exclusion of aliens, and just like any other group I'm sure there are ways to help aliens with housing and jobs and such. And if there aren't, I can find a way to get one started with L-Corp support."

     

     

    Kara nodded. "Just let me know if you need my help."

     

     

    After agreeing, the blonde changed the topicso that Lena could let her ideas stew in the back of her mind rather than continue to be the focus of conversation. She was grateful, because otherwise she'd have likely become hyperfocused on the topic and had to start making calls and plans immediately, and Lena wanted to be present at this table with this incredible woman. Her eyes caught Tenar's, and was surprised to find approval in the Elvari's gaze. 

     

     

    Drinks were brought over, and Lena eyed the glowing amber concoction in front of Kara with a mixture of concern and curiosity. Seeing her interest, Kara flushed a little and said, "Um, it's one of the only brews that can affect a Krytponian's system long enough for there to be a buzz. My metabolism usually breaks down anything else so quickly that I barely get to have that. It is, however, deadly to humans, so while I'm sureyou are curious to taste it I'm going to have to ask that you not."

     

     

    "Shame," Lena commented with a grin. "Though I wouldn't mind doing tests on it sometime. Why does it glow?"

     

     

    Kara shrugged, not knowing, but Tenar was able to answer. "One of the main ingredients is a phosphorescent mushroom native only to the caves of Aldebaran, which is why it is called Aldebaran rum. It is possible to grow it off planet now, but at one time the only way to get it what to have it exported from that planet."

     

     

    "Krypton didn't actually import any spirits," Kara added, "nor did we make any. It was considered taboo to be intellectually compromised by any substances like that. And considering we're genetically engineered, we thought ourselves above the need for such things."

     

     

    "And yet you're drinking now?"

     

     

    "Well, yeah. I'm not on Krypton anymore, for starters," the blonde pointed out. "Besides, I've learned a lot about my people in the last year or so that I decided that I should let myself indulge in some things. Besides, sex was basically taboo as well, and I'd already decided to ignore that custom. Why not drink once in a while?"

     

     

    Lena was stuck on the fact that Kryptonians didn't have sex. "How did you reproduce?"

     

     

    "Tedt tube babies," Alex told her simply, her tone implying that she was using that phrasing to annoy her sister.

     

     

    And sure enough, Kara huffed with indignation. "Alex! We weren't created in a test tube! The birthing pods were artificial wombs, not glass vials," she pointed out.

     

     

    "And before we end up going down that rabbit hole," Tenar interrupted, "can we please change the subject? While Lena is likely fascinated by the idea of test tube babies because she's a science nerd, it's extremely weird for the rest of us."

     

     

    Food arriving helped them segue into other topics of discussion, Alex complaining after she had tried to take something from one of Kara's plates only to be slapped away by her sister while Lena could take bits from any of Kara's meals without reprimand. 

     

     

    "I'm your sister!"

     

     

    "She's her shesur:zhaol," Tenar countered for the Kryptonian. 

     

     

    Finally, her curiosity was too much, and Lena could help but ask. "What does that word mean?"

     

    Chapter End Notes

    As for a mental health update...

    I spent 24 hours at the crisis unit. I'm not better, but it left me with a much brighter outlook and greater appreciation for my life.     Chapter 8

    Chapter Notes

    The silence following her question was concerning, and her curiosity quickly transformed to a tight ball of apprehension growing in the pit of her stomach. Was it not actually something positive? It had seemed like a big deal, and not something bad, but she didn't really know and the fact that no one was answering-

With Krypton's destruction I believed it to be my fate to never find my other half."

The nagging voice in the back of her head that said that it was too good to be true was pushed aside for now, to be obsessed over later when she was home alone to fret over everything.     Chapter 9

    Chapter Notes

    I have a plan!     It will not be as expected.

    Kara's apartment was both exactly what Lena would have envisioned and yet not at the same time. It was a spacious studio and the blonde had done what she could to make it seem larger than it was.     The rest of their visit was largely uneventful, at least in regards to social situations. Lena was endlessly amused by how Kara acted at each exibit and enclosure, even speaking Kryptonian at times to the animals when she just got so excited about something they were seeing. Because Kara had to eat so much, they would buy food at every stall they came across, Lena ceding to let Kara pay for her periodic meals in exchange for letting her buy lunch, where Kara could get several things at once, at the food court area and dinner at the zoo's restaurant. When Kara would offer her bites, she would take them even if she wasn't really that interested in the item.

     

    It was after lunch that she finally asked about the food sharing.

     

    "It's not necessarily just about food," Kara explained as they moved away from the food court and back towards the animals. "When Kryptonians really enjoy something, we find joy is sharing it with our mates. I know you aren't technically my mate yet, and if you never want to be that is fine, but it's still something I like to do. It's why I love hearing about what you do at work even if I don't have quite the love for science that I did as a child. You love it and are really passionate about it, and so I will always want you to share it when you want to. The food on Earth is one of the things I love about the people on this planet, which is good since I need to eat so much of it. It's so diverse and amazing, with all the flavors and the way the same ingredients can be different based on preparations and cooking. It's fascinating!"

     

    That made a lot of sense, and Lena made a note to buy a lot of cookbooks as well as find some classes for foreign cuisine. She may never be able to do a lot of the dishes justice, but she did enjoy cooking and now that she knows that Kara takes such joy in it, it would be worth the time that those classes would take. She may even ask if Kara wanted to do them with her, since it would be a way of sharing a passion.

     

    "On Krypton, most of our food was synthetic. We had large greenhouses, so we usually could get fresh fruits and vegetables fairly easily, especially thr higher Houses like mine, but meat was a bit harder and more rare. We didn't have any natural water sources left so fish were even more difficult to get as well. Coming from that to this planet with all its variations, it was a shock for me at first. But then I fell in love with it, so I share it. It's not easy for me to share food with anyone because it's such an intimate thing for me, so Alex will probably complain about it every time she sees it, but I do share pizza with her."

     

    "Does she not know about this part of your culture?"

     

    Kara shook her head. "The Danvers are amazing, and I'm so lucky to have them. But they were more concerned with me fitting in and being human than understanding my culture. And by the time I was able to blend in, it just felt like too much time had passed to start sharing it."

     

    That made sense, but Lena had a feeling that Alex would be willing to listen. Maybe she wouldn't have when they were growing up, but the bond she saw now made it easy for her to think that the redhead would be receptive to it now.

     

    "Maybe you should talk about it the next time she complains about you not sharing food with her."

     

    Kara was quiet as she thought about that, and after a couple minutes she nodded. "Yeah. After she saw what I had when I was trapped by the Black Mercy, she was different about my alienness. She'd probably accept it a lot more now."

     

    "Black Mercy?"

     

    That diverted their topics onto what happened with the parasitic plant, and then moved onto Kara's aunt and what she found out about her mother, how she was conflicted by what her memories told her and what she learned. Lena spoke of how she was never quite good enough for her own mother, for Lillian, and how she was always much closer to her father. Eventually they moved to eating dinner at the restaurant, using the director's promise from earlier to get a more private table. Their waiter was discrete and understanding, though he did ask for a photo with them both after learning they were on a date today and explaining he was gay and starting to come out.

     

    "Knowing that you two are queer to some degree is inspirational," he told them.

     

    After dinner they perused the gift shop, and Kara suggested they each buy something for the other, jokingly saying, "Under fifty, Lena!" as they walked inside.

     

    "No promises," was Lena's response, though she would make an effort not to if she didn't find something amazing that was over the price. Since Kara just smiled and shook her head but didn't complain, she figured that was permission enough if she did find something.

     

    Eventually she found a lion hooded blanket, having been impressed by Kara's current collection and this one felt so soft that she just knew Kara would love it. And since Kara only gave a spending limit and not a number of items limit, she couldn't help herself with she saw a t-shirt with a roaring lion and the word "hangry" on it.

     

    Kara was already outside the shop when she finished, and they shyly exchanged gifts. Lena didn't have to look in a bag to see what Kara had purchased for her, taking the jumbo otter plush with hidden glee. It was wearing a Supergirl t-shirt, which made it all the more special for her. As Kara looked at her items, she mock frowned at the fact there were two things, but once she saw them she could help but laugh happily. And she even pulled the blanket onto her head to wrap around herself.

     

    "Ooo, this is so soft!"

     

    "I noticed you seemed to have a lot of softer fabrics," Lena told her, reaching out to help arrange the blanket so it sat better over her shoulders, "and that you seemed to really like blankets."

     

    "I do," Kara confirmed, nodding. "I don't need them for temperature fluctuations, but I like the feeling of them on me. I have a weighted blanket I sometimes use. It's technically several of them so that I can feel it that Eliza made for me when I was a teen. It's been redone a few times, with new covering, but I like to use it when I dont quite make it for a save."

     

    "I've used a weighted blanket before. I still have one, I think, though I couldn't tell you whether it was at my penthouse or my home." Lena frowned, trying to think of when she last saw it bur eventually shrugging it off. "There's a lot of research about it that I thought might help me, but it just made me feel trapped."

     

    "I was afraid it'd feel like that for me as well, but luckily it doesn't. That might be because it feels like a light blanket would have back home, though."

     

    It was time to join Frank now, and they moved to the entrance where he was waiting with his family. LeAnn looked exhausted, which was never a bad thing with kids since it meant good sleep overnight usually. They went to Kara's apartment first, and Lena stepped out so that she could say good-night.

     

    "It feels a little strange to know I won't be sleeping next to you tonight," she admitted as they paused at the building's door. "At the same time, I think it will be good to have a little space. I don't think everything, from the reveal of both your identity and your mark, has completely sank in yet."

     

    Kara nodded. "I understand that. I'll still message you, but I'm going to let you determine when we see each other again so you can have the time you need. Today was a lot of fun, and I'm really glad we went out." She hesitated for a moment, and then stepped a little closer. "Could I kiss you, Lena?"

     

    "Absolutely."

     

    Their lips touched tentatively at first, but then Lena pressed closer and put her hands on Kara's shoulders. It was almost natural how Kara, her hands still holding the edges of her hooded blanket, went to Lena's lower back and pulled her closer. The blonde heard several clicks of a camera, or she probably wouldn't have pulled back.

     

    "Photographer," she murmured, not really fully separated.

     

    Lena hummed. "I guess the tabloids will be having a good day tomorrow. How much do you wanna bet at least two will be calling us great friends?"

     

    Laughter had Kara pulling back a little further. Then, trying to sound scandalized, she said, Miss Luthor! Are you trying to seduce me into doing something as bad as gambling?"

     

    "I don't know about gambling," the brunette teased, "but seduce wouldn't be a bad term for what I'd like to do." They giggled, both a little pink and maybe more than a little drunk off the kiss. Not that it stopped them from coming together again, kissing softly but no less passionately. "I should go so Frank can get home for the evening."

     

    Kara nodded. "Right. I'll talk to you later?"

     

    "Definitely."

    Chapter End Notes

    So the majority of what was commented seemed against Mon-El unless there was a good plot reason. The only plot reason I had was that there was a very convoluted reason he was actually her soulmate, not Lena, and then he got captured like in the show so that Lillian could get Kara to come and "donate" her blood. In the end, if he makes an appearance it will be because I choose to use the invasion in some way and not as relationship drama. And since he doesn't really have a compelling reason to stay due to Kara, I'm not sure how that will come up if I even decide to use it. I'd have to decide if he even left the Well of Stars (or wherever he went with his pod) to begin with to lead the Daxamites to Earth.

    Honestly, the only reason the invasion is even enticing is because of that dress. You know the one.

    I think I have a better way of handling the second part of my reason to use Mon-El, because I didn't use certain elements yet that I could use that were part of S2. And while I want to use canon, I don't want to use it in a way that is always canon compliant or even canon adjacent. For example, the "Lillian is leader of Cadmus and evil" aspect would be nice to use. Even including the Medusa virus, which is why I was considering including Mon-El, was something I am trying to plan for. But how those things are revealed or unfold? Yeah, canon doesn't matter for that.

    Just a reminder than everything up to the speech Lena gives (bombing L-Corp and the stage, Corben fighting Alex, Lena shooting Corben) has happened, though some details may be a little different. Other than that, if I haven't included it in the fic, it hasn't happened. And I only rewatched S2's episodes regarding Medusa, not anything else, as research for this fic.

    Chat Room 1

    Chapter Summary

    Superfriends chat room

    Chapter Notes

    On Monday, Lena heard her phone alert and checked it to find a message telling her she was added to a chat called SuperFriends and opened it to see what it was. She didn't know who all of these people were, though Agent was probably Alex and KDawg was probably Kara. Winn'r and Guardian were unfamiliar, but TenTen was likely Tenar, and Lena wonder who named everyone because she couldn't see the Elvari choosing that name. She looked over to see what she had been labeled as.

    Chapter 12

    Chapter Notes

    I'm not entirely sure how I feel about this chapter. Because I decided against Mon-El, I moved up the timeline a bit since I won't have to have a few chapters to develop his role in this, and it meant I needed a different idiot to do the thing.

    Don't worry, though. The idiot will probably redeem himself. Maybe.

    For two weeks after their zoo date, Lena stayed in touch with Kara digitally but they didn't meet up again. She had wanted to, but one of the fights near the port ended up damaging one of L-Corp's warehouses and the board increased their push for anti-alien products and policies within the company. She'd already had to deal with their vitriol for being in the tabloids kissing Supergirl ("though at least she looks human," one had said at the time - like Tenar wasn't just as beautiful were Lena not so fascinated with the blonde now that she had exposure) and so she was trying to stay firm on her stance with L-Corp's policies and trying to push for pro-alien products in response to their anti-alien ones.

     

     

    It meant neither would happen, not with the current board, but maybe if she pushed enough she would lose some of them and open up space for new members who would accept her products and policies. An ally on the board would be nice, for once. The only reason she got away with this sort of pushback was only because she owned majority shares to the company thanks to Lex's being given to her upon his incarceration. 

     

     

    So when Friday night after those two weeks came up, Lena closed down her computer at five and chose to leave behind the stack of files in favor of spending as much time not thinking of the company as much as she could. A text to Kara let her know that the blonde was with the rest of the self-proclaimed "SuperFriends" at the alien bar so she had Frank come around to take her out there. She didn't even bother changing, wanting only to be with her girlfriend right now even if it meant she was still in office wear.

     

     

    Half hour later, Frank was pulling up our front and Kara was walking over to open the door for her after waiting outside once Lena texted. "Hey. Gosh, it's been too long," she told her, leaning in and hugging her a little tightly.

     

     

    "It has," Lena breathed, closing her eyes and letting herself just soak up her presence. "I think I'm going to have to institute a no-work weekends for every week unless there's a product launch coming up."

     

     

    "Hey, I'm all for that."

     

     

    "Thought you might." With a sigh, she pulled away and leaned down to tell Frank, "I'm going to go home with Kara tonight so you don't need to stick around."

     

     

    Frank smiled warmly and nodded. "Of course, Miss Luthor. I trust she'll.keep you safe."

     

     

    "Alway," Kara promised him. Then they backed up and he drove off, leaving them alone on the sidewalk. "Let's go inside. You haven't met Winn and James yet, and Alex invited a detective she has a crush on but thinks I haven't noticed. It's absolutely adorable. The boys haven't figured it out either, thinks they're just professional friends starting to become better friends."

     

     

    "This should be fun to watch then," Lena commented with a grin.

     

     

    "Oh, definitely. If Maggie hadn't found a way to come out to them pretty quickly, I think Winn would have tried his luck. He looked about ready to when she joined us. It was great."

     

     

    Lena chuckled and took Kara's hand as they entered the bar, letting the blonde lead them to a new table. This one wasn't as secluded, but it was large enough for their entire group and that was necessary at this point. The surface was already covered in food, though mostly appetizers that the others were sharing and their entrees.

     

     

    "You didn't order yet?" Lena asked, looking to Kara who shook her head.

     

     

    "She wouldn't order until you got here," Winn explained. "Which is really weird because Kara lives and breathes food."

     

     

    "I had a couple wings to tide me over," Kara commented as she sat down next to Alex, Lena sliding in next to her. The blonde turned to her so she looked back. "I wanted to see if there was anything you wanted me to add or anything you didn't like that I usually get."

     

     

    "You never change your order," James pointed out, sounding a little dumbfounded. "You don't change your orders and you don't share. Those are the rules for you with food."

     

     

    Lena realized then that Kara hadn't explained the cultural significance to them and wondered why, but decided not to ask here. If Kara had a reason for it, then Lena wouldn't force the issue. "Um. I haven't actually looked at the full menu yet so I'm not sure. I haven't disliked anything you've gotten before, though it's a little pork heavy. Maybe more chicken dishes and something with fish? I haven't eaten since I had a breakfast smoothie, so I'm famished."

     

     

    "That's not healthy," Kara pointed out, taking the laminated two page menu that Alex handed her to let Lena take a look at what was offered. "There are three alien dishes available tonight as well. I'll be adding one of them, a Starhaven native dish. It's vegan, as they don't eat meat, and I thought you'd really enjoy it. The other two should be safe for humans, but one if the ingredients used in both haven't been tested yet so I'm not comfortable with you trying them. They hadn't been able to get it before yesterday so both dishes are really new."

     

     

    "I'd love to try the Starhaven dish," Lena told her.

     

     

    "My father took me there once. It's a beautiful planet, one akin to the fantasy art I see online sometimes. I wish I could show you... Maybe I'll try painting one of my favorite landmarks for you. It probably won't do the view justice, but it would at least let you have a glimpse."

     

     

    Lena smiled and leaned into her side. "I'd love to see it, if you're willing to show me."

     

     

    James was watching with a furrowed expression, and at Lena's action said, "What is going on with you two, anyway?"

     

     

    A Latina woman approached then, not letting then answer as everyone's attention moved to her, and the boys stood so she could slide through to sit next to Alex. "What did I miss? Besides being joined by two people," she asked, her expression open and friendly. 

     

     

    Alex cleared her throat. "This is Lena Luthor and Kara Danvers, my sister. And her girlfriend. Lena, Kara, meet Maggie. She's a detective for NCPD's science division."

     

     

    "Nice to meet you," Maggie told them before her gaze settled on Kara. "And you're the sister? Alex has told me about you. Of course, everyone knows who you are now ever since that gala with the hotel nearly coming down."

     

     

    Kara offered an uneasy smile. "Yup, that's me."

     

     

    "Didn't realize you were gay. Alex didn't tell me about that, and it's something that hasn't been published. Not anywhere reputable, anyway."

     

     

    Tabloids did in fact shove a picture of them kissing, wrapped up together with the lion hooded blanket around them both. Only one had dared to suggest they were just gals being pals, while half of the others wondered if they were pulling some sort of publicity stunt to "get the gays." It was annoying to Lena, but she didn't let herself linger on it long. All that mattered was that she and Kara knew the truth.

     

     

    And that Cat Grant had demanded an interview with them both for the exclusive. Thankfully Lena had been too busy the last couple weeks, but she wouldn't put it off forever. Cat was Kara's boss, after all. Too much delay may not cause Kara to be fired, but it could make work uncomfortable if Cat kept bothering her about it.

     

     

    "It's not so much that I'm gay as I don't use human labels. Krypton didn't have that sexuality segregation, didn't have a word to even suggest it even," Kara told her, arm around Lena's waist as they both looked at the menu. "I suppose if I had to have a label, pansexual would be accurate."

     

     

    Maggie took the hint and dropped the subject, turning to James who inadvertently had his question answered and looked like he wasn't happy with the response. "You good, Olsen?"

     

     

    "Yeah," he bit, looking down from practically glaring at Kara. It was obvious he wasn't, but they were gracious enough not to call him out on it. After a moment, he went back to eating on his burger and fries.

     

     

    Lena pointed at a few things, Kara nodding at most of them and explaining anything unfamiliar to her. After a few minutes she had an idea of what to order and Lena stood to let her out before sliding in next to Alex and finally letting herself just relax fully.

     

     

    "Long day?" Alex asked, softening a little as she spoke to the brunette. While she'd been wary of the Luthor early on, she was starting to accept her presence in Kara's life. The fact that Kara told her about how Lena had encouraged her to talk about her culture more with Alex definitely helped. Alex had assumed so much that hearing the truth about Kara's life before she landed on Earth was enlightening. 

     

     

    "Long week. Two, to be fair," Lena told her, rolling her neck. "I came straight from the office, as I'm sure you can tell, and didn't even pack up work to do this weekend. I'm not sure what I'll do yet, but I want to head out to the house and hope Kara will come with me tonight. She loves flying so I was going to give her a chance to take me home by SuperAir if she was up to it."

     

     

    Alex chuckled. "I bet she will be. She's been wanting to take you flying, but she knows some people are freaked out by heights so she won't ask."

     

     

    "I suppose its a good thing I plan to make the suggestion." Lena had been thinking about this a lot, especially after learning about how much Kryptonians showed affection through the sharing of passions. She didn't want Kara to have to deny something just because it scared her, and she knew she could trust Kara to keep her safe. Most evenings if they called each other, the superhero would be out flying just to fly and Lena thought that maybe Kara hadn't invited her on a flight because of the heights issue. It didn't help that the blonde had been firm in her decision to not make suggestions about seeing each other unless Lena did first, and Lena wouldn't let herself with the way work had been going.

     

     

    "I suppose you're all right, Luthor." Alex said it so fondly that Lena was surprised she didn't have her usual reaction to being called by her last name. If it isn't said with hate or anger, it wasn't so bad.

     

     

    Kara slid into the seat then and immediately slid her arm around Lena, pressing a kiss to her temple. "M'gann put the order in. She'll be bring you a Scotch as soon as she gets the ticket to the kitchen and pours one."

     

     

    "Thank you." Lena pressed into her side and glanced at the drinks on the table. "Not going to drink your alien alcohol tonight?"

     

     

    Kara shook her head, and Lena smirked when she realized her cheeks were flushed. "No, um, I kinda hoped I'd get to see you and if you didnt contact me until later, I didn't want to be delayed in flying to you quickly and safely."

     

     

    "That's adorable, anam cara." 

     

     

    "You're adorable, shesur:zhaol."

     

     

    "Ugh," Alex groaned. "Is this how it's going to be with you two? Seriously?"

     

     

    "Oh, definitely," Kara answered, looking very serious as she nodded. "Forever, Alex. And only around you. You know how it is."

     

     

    "Oh god." Alex laid her head on the table before hitting her forehead against the surface. "I think I need to tell Mom to disown me."

     

     

    "Why not disown Kara?" Winn asked. When the sisters glared at him, he raised his hands. "Just asking. She is the adopted one, after all."

     

     

    "Yeah, but she's the favorite," Alex pointed out.

     

     

    Kara scoffed. "I was not the favorite. Just because they paid more attention to me doesn't mean I'm the favorite."

     

     

    "Uh-huh."

     

     

    "It was only because I was the more exceptional one, that's all," Kara clarified in an exaggeratedly conceited tone, making everyone laugh including Alex.

     

     

    "You were exceptional, all right," Alex told her with a grin, still laughing. "Exceptionally bad, Kara."

     

     

    They devolved into swapping stories about who was worse as a teen then, and Lena just let herself relax into Kara while she listened. She hadn't even realized how much she needed this until just now, and she decided that even if she had to work over the weekend that she needed to join them at least once a week.

     

     

    The stories stopped only when the food arrived, and shortly after Tenar approached the table so everyone slid around so she could sit next to Kara. Lena watched them for a moment, taking in their easy intimacy born of being lovers for a time, but found that she wasn't really jealous anymore. Kara kept one arm around her and was eating with the other even as Tenar leaned into her for a moment to take in the comfort of that touch until her drink was delivered and she sat up completely again. James had shot the Elvari a dirty look when she had done that, and Lena quickly realized that the man was jealous. She didn't really blame him, but she could dislike his attitude and decided she would be polite but disinterested in him unless he stopped being so rude about the fact that Kara didn't choose him.

     

     

    She wasn't surprised when he tried to take something from one of Kara's plates a few minutes later only to have a strong hand clamp onto his wrist.

     

     

    "Ow! What the hell, Kara?" he exclaimed, yanking at his wrist ineffectively before looking at her and seeing her frown and narrowed eyes. "What's the big deal? Someone you barely know can share your food but your friends for years can't?"

     

     

    "That's not the right way to speak of Lena," Tenar warned him softly. "The shesur:zhaol is sacred for Kryptonians."

     

     

    "If it's such a big deal, why would Clark share food?"

     

     

    Lena blinked at that admission and sighed internally. She could feel Alex do the same, only more audibly, and the two exchanged knowing glances.

     

     

    "Kal doesn't have a shesur:zhaol, nor was he raised to understand how sacred one is," Kara told him, tone even and still holding his wrist. From his pained expression, she was taking care not to break anything but she wasn't being so gentle as not to likely bruise. "I'm not like Kal, James. He's more human than Kryptonian, and that's okay. But that means that when I tell you it's a Kryptonian thing and you try to ignore it anyway, to ignore my culture, is rude and insulting."

     

     

    She finally let go of his wrist and he pulled it to his chest to rub it with his other hand. "What makes Lena this shesur thing anyway?"

     

     

    "Shesur:zhaol." Kara enunciated it slowly so he could hear it properly, and Lena said it quietly several times to make sure she could say it correctly as well. The light squeeze she got from Kara's arm around her waist when she said it right had her feeling warm and pleased. "If you had asked out of curiosity and not anger, I might have explained. As it is, it's too important for me and you will likely not treat it as seriously as it is. The fact that Lena being my girlfriend wasn't enough of a reason for her to eat from my plate already tells me enough."

     

     

    James scowled and stood. "I suppose that tells me enough about how much our friendship means to you."

     

     

    "If your actions were about friendship and not jealousy," Lena drawled, "then I would have been more inclined to encourage Kara to explain it to you. But you just can't stand not being the one she picked, can you?"

     

     

    "Wait, is that true?" Winn asked, turning to his friend. "Come on, James. She tried, remember? You guys went on one date, one!     James growled as he tripped over a garbage bag and slammed his side into the brick wall. After regaining his balance, as much as he could with as drunk as he was, he kicked the bag before walking again with his hands in his pockets.

     

     

    "Mr. Olsen?" called out someone behind him, and he turned quickly which caused him to loose his balance again. Two sets of arms grabbed him and helped him steady, and he nodded his thanks. "Mr. Olsen, I have a proposition for you."

     

     

    James squinted at the speaker. Something seemed familiar about the man, but his liquor addled mind just couldn't place it. "Yeah? Whatsit to ya?"

     

     

    "We want Lena Luthor."

     

     

    "I'm not interested in helping with an assassination."

     

     

    "No, no. We aren't going to assassinate her. We're here on behalf of her mother. See, she's been trying to reconcile with her ever since Lex's arrest and Lena has refused every effort. But now Mrs. Luthor is ill, and she has asked us to bring her to her. It's just to give our employer a chance, see?"

     

     

    That made sense. If he could have a chance to say good bye to his father, he'd jump at it. Of course Lena was selfish enough to deny her mother a chance.

     

     

    "Whaddya need me to do?"

     

     

    "We just need you to find a way to separate her and Supergirl. In fact, if you could get the hero to a warehouse on the wharf tomorrow afternoon, we can find a way to keep her busy long enough to get Lena so we can take her to her mother."

     

     

    James didn't really care what they did with Lena, honestly. He just didn't want to be there if these guys decided to kill her. "And Kara will be unharmed?"

     

     

    "She may be roughed up a bit, but that's only because we need to make the distraction legit."

     

     

    It wasn't really that hard of a decision for him. If Lena was out of the picture somehow, he could move in to comfort Kara and maybe then she'd see he was a better shesur thing, whatever that was. He left Lucy for her, so why wouldn't she give him an honest chance?

     

     

    "Okay. I'll do it."

     

    Chapter End Notes

    So. I'm not a hater of James, just in case people get mad about what I did to him in this chapter. I find him to be an idiot and self-absorbed, and the show pairing him with Lena was stupid after how adamant he was she was evil for so long, but I don't hate him.

    If James understood the soulmate mark and cultural aspects, he'd be less of an idiot even if he didn't like the pairing. But he doesn't, and that ignorance is why he's being used here. That's all. So if you don't like me making him do a bad thing, just remember it's out of ignorance. And also that I just wasn't sure how to develop the next thing without having someone do this.

It's been fixed and, obviously, finished. But it delayed me a few days.

    Probably a good thing it did, though, because the end of this was not the plan originally (I wasn't sure how to execute that plan anyway) but this ended up so much better.
