It seemed to only frustrate him further when she merely raised a brow at him in response. They listened as Kara called Maggie and spoke to her, and it was clear after a moment that the detective was telling her to let them handle it unless they called for backup. And when Kara relayed that to James, he scowled and turned as he said, "I can't believe what a coward you are, Kara. Clark would have acted."

     

     

    Kara sighed. "Kal also doesn't work with the local authorities as closely as I do, James. He's extremely independent."

     

     

    "Whatever." He stormed down the stairs and slammed the door downstairs open to leave.

     

     

    "That was unpleasant," Lena commented before stepping forward to put an arm around Kara and take hold of one of her hands. "Are you okay? I know he's your friend and it's never a good feeling when one acts like this."

     

     

    "Honestly?" Kara sighed and led them out of her door, turning and locking it behind them. "He came here to make a name for himself and then sought me out because Kal asked him to keep an eye on me. I thought he was cool and I didn't have a lot of contact with my cousin, so the fact that there was someone in front of me who could tell me about him?" She shrugged and offered a small smile. "James is a good guy, and I had thought once that he wouldn't be a bad partner if I couldn't have my soulmate. When we tried to make it work, though, it just felt so weird that I couldn't commit."

     

     

    "And he didn't take it well, I assume?"

     

     

    Kara shook her head. "Not really. He said he needed time, and I let him have it for a while. Then I met you, and Kal was here for a while, and suddenly he was a vigilante..."

     

     

    They stepped outside and started walking towards the Jeep, but before they got too far someone behind them sneered, "Don't you two just look cozy."

     

     

    They turned and Lena inhaled sharply as she took in the man sauntering towards them. She didn't notice Kara start to sway beside her. "John Corben?"

     

     

    "In the flesh. Had some work done too," he told them, grinning widely as he tore his shirt open to expose the green rock in his chest. He was still walking closer, and this time when Kara stumbled backwards Lena noticed. A van pulled up next to them, and he motioned towards it. "Get in, Supergirl."

     

     

    Lena stepped between Kara and Corben. "Like hell."

     

     

    Corben shrugged. "We're not s'posed to kill you, but there was nothing against roughing ya up." 

     

     

    Kara put a hand on Lena's shoulder and stumbled forward. "I'll get in, just don't hurt her."

     

     

    "I think we'll take both of ya, to make sure you stay in line." Another man came up behind them and grabbed Lena, and even as she moved around trying to break his hold he was too strong. Kara tried to help, but Corben was too close and she was too weak. She could barely stand anymore by the time the assassin grabbed her and tossed her into the van.

     

     

    They were blindfolded and cuffed to a bar along the wall of the van, and since Kara could feel the kryptonite burning through her still she knew Corben was close. She gritted her teeth and tried to use her x-ray vision so she could follow their path but only ended up making the incoming migraine worse. The feeling of Lena next her to, her head falling on the brunette's shoulder, was as much a comfort as it was a fear.

     

     

    What were these people going to do with them?

     

    Chapter End Notes

    James will (maybe) come around.

    By the way, Maggie or Kelly? Or Sam?

    Chapter 14

    Chapter Notes

    I'm apparently not going to leave a long cliffhanger for y'all.

    Enjoy!

    The van doors opened into a large room that was partly underground, a handful of high small windows in a few places letting in only a sliver of sunlight. Lena had to help Kara move when they told them to get out, Corben standing close and laughing at the blonde's struggles. She supposed she should be thankful he didn't decide to be the one who carried her out of the van. 

     

     

    They were led down a set of stairs into another room with a cage and Lena was shoved inside, losing her balance and falling into the space while Kara was ripped away by Corben. 

     

     

    "Lena!" Kara choked out, before turning to glare at the man who had shoved her. She was in too much pain to do anything, but she'd remember his face.

     

     

    "The boss needs you to be human for a bit," the assassin told her, letting her fall to the ground just outside the cage. "We could get what we want now, but your blood would be tainted by the radiation and she doesn't think that will work for her, see?"

     

     

    One of the goons walked over with some sort of contraption, smirking at her. 

     

     

    "She said to make you use your heat rays into thei helmet til you run out of juice, or else we shoot the hostage." He grinned at Lena. "She don't know who the hostage is, but considering my contract is still out with brother dear I don't mind getting the extra payday."

     

     

    "Fine," Kara croaked, which was followed by Lena saying, "Kara don't!"

     

     

    But the blonde pushed herself to her feet and let them put the device on her head, and she gathered all the anger to put extra power into it, screaming as she let her heat vision go into the glass cover with Corben standing on the other side.

     

     

    Eventually it dissipated and she stumbled, and while she still felt the kryptonite it wasn't as painful now that it wasn't interfering with the solar energy and just attacking her systems that stored and used it. It was more like having the flu than anything worse now.

     

     

    "Grab her."

     

     

    "Kara!"

     

     

    Kara wanted to say she remembered the entirety of what happened in the room they dragged her too. She knew they strapped her down, cut a few strands of hair, and cut into her arm with a knife, just a little, to make sure she was human. A tall woman with her hair in a tight bun wearing a mask and carrying a needle that was used to draw blood was the last thing she remembered clearly. There was more, she knew, because she had cuts and bruises she didn't have before, when she came to in the cell with Lena, her head on the brunette's lap and tears falling from green eyes.

     

     

    "Hey," Kara said quietly, watching as relief washed over Lena's features.

     

     

    "Hey yourself," she responded. "I was so scared they were going to kill you."

     

     

    Kara had been too, but she didn't want Lena to know that. Not when they hadn't gotten out of here yet. "You can't get rid of me that easily."

     

     

    That earned her a quick bark of a laugh, Lena shaking her head at her. "I think when I design your new suit I'm going to add some sort of anti-kryptonite protocol."

     

     

    "Just make sure there's a back door," Kara told her, eyes closing. "You weren't in National City when I got infected with RedK. So long as you can do something in case I get hit with something else that makes me mean, I would absolutely love not feeling the effects of kryptonite."

     

     

    "Rest some more. I don't know when or if they'll come back, but you should try to regain your strength."

     

     

    Kara just hummed before she dozed, not letting herself sleep deeply so she could still hear if anything changed. It was a while before something did, someone shuffling into the room and walking over to their cage. She felt Lena hold onto her tightly, leaned over a little to get a better hold on her. If they weren't in such a bad situation, the press of breasts on her face would have been nice.

     

     

    "You aren't taking her again," Lena snarled.

     

     

    "I'm getting you out of here. You can trust me, Lena Luthor."

     

     

    That was a familiar voice. Kara opened her eyes and saw a man in a hoodie with the hood up standing in the cage's open doorway. "Jeremiah?" 

     

     

    The man pushed off his hoodie and smiled sadly. "Hey, kiddo. I see you're having a hard time today."

     

     

    Kara barked out a harsh laugh. "That's a nice way to say I look like crap, right?"

     

     

    "You know him?" Lena asked quietly.

     

     

    "Jeremiah Danvers," the blonde told her, "Alex's father and my adopted one. He was taken instead of me when the anti-alien DEO came to get me. They found me because I'd been flying in the area and thought it was fine."

     

     

    "I made that decision," Jeremiah told her, coming closer and crouching a little, "and I'd make it again every time, Kara."

     

     

    Between him and Lena they managed to her the blonde to her feet. She was a little dizzy at first, but after a moment she was able to stand and walk without a lot of assistance. Jeremiah led them down several hallways until they came to a locked gate that he used a card to open just as alarms started blaring.

     

     

    "Shit." Jeremiah pushed them through and closed the gate quickly, him on the inside. "Go. This hall opens up to the outside. I'll go keep them off your trail a bit longer."

     

     

    "Wait, you should come with us!"

     

     

    Jeremiah smiled sadly. "I've been here almost fifteen years already, kiddo. I can handle a little longer. Just tell Alex I love her and I'm so proud of her."

     

     

    It was obvious Kara knew this was the best option as she deflated and nodded, and Lena vowed to find a way to help her get the man who became her father back somehow.

     

     

    The actual escape was a little blurry, Kara still too dizzy to really keep up with everything happening. If it weren't dark out, the full force of the sun would have helped push off some of the remaining effects of the kryptonite even if she didn't get her powers back right away, but she wasn't so lucky. 

     

     

    Lena had hot wired a car for them about as quickly as she could find one and drove them back into National City. Unfortunately she didn't know where the DEO was, and she was going to have words with Alex about that for potential future situations like this, so she pulled up to a bar that was still open and asked to use their phone.

     

     

    "Alex?"

     

     

    "Lena? Where the hell are you? James called me hours ago and said you'd been nabbed by Corben and he had kryptonite."

     

     

    She was going to keep going, so Lena quickly interrupted. "Alex. Jeremiah was there, he let us out. If you hurry-"

     

     

    That got the redhead to stop her worried fretting and get to business. After passing along the known location of the hideout and getting the downtown DEO headquarters address in exchange so that Kara could get an exam, Lena hung up and, after thanking thr bartender, left to finish driving before her adrenaline crashed and she was too tired to do so safely.

     

     

    Alex was their Kryptonian doctor and she'd gone to the site to try and rescue her father. Lena tried not to be upset with her over that, especially since the doctor who was there did everything she was comfortable with. She started the Kryptonian on fluids with a low dose painkiller since she was basically human right now. Pictures were taken of every injury and wound, and the ones that could be taken care of were. Then they were taken to a separate room where Kara was set up under some giant lights that the doctor turned on low so Lena could sit with her.

     

     

    "When Agent Danvers returns she may turn these up," Doctor Hamilton warned her before leaving, "and if she does they are too strong for humans to be in direct line of, so be very careful."

     

     

    James had apparently grabbed their bags that had been left behind, and Lena felt angry that he hadn't jumped in to try and help them when it happened. They were all human, though she wasn't sure about Corben entirely, and he was all about fighting humans in his armor. Why he would just stand by and let Kara get taken...

     

     

    Apparently as Kara's soulmate she had privileges and rights at the DEO similar to a spouse, which she learned when James tried to push into the room around her and agents helped remove him when she denied him entrance.

     

     

    "Soulmates?" he asked, incredulous, after one of the agents explained to Lena why they were helping remove him. "That's not a thing. What the hell are you guys being brainwashed with here?"

     

     

    "It is a thing," Winn told him as he walked through, "for Kryptonians. Dude, I told you to apologize and listen to Kara. She would have told you all this if you just talked to her like I said."

     

     

    "But Clark-"

     

     

    "Is not Kara," Lena snapped. "God, why do men always think that women have to follow the same rules as their male family members? Of course, then they try to claim we aren't as good as them either. Really, James, are you actually Kara's friend? Or do you think you're her keeper, thinking her cousin gave you some kind of power over her?"

     

     

    James puffed up and snarled out, "He told me to watch out for her."

     

     

    "To help her keep her secret," a firm voice said, Superman walking over. He looked at Lena and nodded once as his mouth twisted into that charming smile she saw everywhere in Metropolis. "Miss Luthor. Thank you for looking out for my cousin. I'm sorry I'm late. When Alex told me about Jeremiah, I went to help them first since time was of the essence there. They'll be here in the next fifteen minutes."

     

     

    "Thank you, Superman, for the update," Lena told him politely. After an awkward moment, she realized that he and Winn were waiting for her permission to see Kara. Clearing her throat and ignoring the slight heat to her cheeks, she stepped back and waved at the door. "Would you and Winn like to see her?"

     

     

    Superman smiled wider and nodded. "So long as you're okay with it. I didn't know about the soulmate thing, likely because I didn't have the mark and so my little bit of Kryptonian education didn't include it when I first set up the Fortress. I don't want to step on any toes."

     

     

    Lena offered him a warmer smile than she had before. "Thank you, but I know she'd love your company."

     

     

    "I wish I'd taken more time with her growing up," he admitted quietly as he stepped closer. He put a hand on Lena's shoulder as he passed, saying, "You're good for her, Miss Luthor."

     

     

    "Lena, please." He went in, and she turned to Winn and laughed at his starstruck expression. "You know a superhero and you still fangirl over her cousin?"

     

     

    Winn snapped out of it and pouted a little. "I can't help it! Kara is just... Kara. I mean, I know she can do everything he can, and some of it even better, but like... It's Superman! He's a childhood icon for me."

     

     

    It warmed her to know Winn still thought of Kara as just one of his friends. She knew that the blonde worried they didn't see past her cape, but this was proof that Winn, at least, still just saw her. "Well, why don't you join him and visit with your friend?"

     

     

    "I want to give them a little time alone," he told her softly, his smile warm and understanding. "Kara has always kinda missed him, you know? She told me once she understands he's busy, and now that she has the cape she gets why he didn't visit more, but it's always bothered her how little she really knows him beyond his heroics on TV."

     

     

    It was such a nice thing that Lena decided to sit on a bench with him just outside the room and talk for a while. They both had seen James loitering at the end of the hall, though they weren't sure if it was to talk to Superman or to find some other way to get in and see Kara, maybe hoping she'd want to see him when she woke up.

     

     

    "We saw you and Tenar talking closely last night," she teased him after a while when he started bemoaning his lack of dating life.

     

     

    "She had offered to share her bed with me, and you saw her explaining Elvari relationships to me when you left. I didn't, in case you were curious, but we did hang out and play Call of Duty last night," he explained to her. "I don't think a hookup would be bad, but I guess I want to at least have the illusion that it could go somewhere, you know? She was really understanding about it, which was why we ended up just gaming so we could talk more about it. I think she knows some people who might be interested so she was getting some details to pass along too."

     

     

    "Alien blind dating?"

     

     

    "I guess? It's nice of her anyway. Tenar is really cool when you get past the whole having-slept-with-Kara thing. You know I kissed her once? Kara, I mean."

     

     

    Lena shook her head. "No, I'm afraid we haven't talked too much about past relationships yet."

     

     

    Winn nodded in understanding. "You guys have only just started dating and stuff, so I guess that makes sense. But yeah, I kissed her and she rejected me. I was her best friend and she didn't want to try anything just to hurt me when she couldn't feel as strongly as I could about it. That's when she told me about the soulmate thing, and it made sense. I was hurt for a while, of course, but I got it. Honestly, I'm more hurt at this point that she's been hiding that bar from me for years. They make amazing chicken wings."

     

     

    They talked a little longer before Superman came back out of the room, his shoulders relaxed. "She's awake and asking for you, Miss- Lena." He turned to Winn and said, "I doubt she'd be opposed to you going in with her. I think she mostly just needs to see that Lena is all right."

     

     

    "Thank you," Lena told him, earning a nod before the man walked over to James. They started talking quietly, and Lena tugged Winn along to go see Kara and let the two men talk without an audience. Maybe James will straighten up after a long talk with Clark, though Lena wouldn't hold her breath.

     

     

    "Hey, guys," Kara greeted them a little weakly. "I gotta say, the lap pillow I had in the cell was way more comfortable."

     

     

    "Funny," Lena deadpanned, though she did slide into the top of the bed so that Kara could rest her head on her lap anyway.

     

     

    "Glad you're okay," Winn told her softly. "I'm also really sorry about James. He saw you guys get nabbed apparently too and didn't do anything except call Alex shortly after you guys got tossed into the van."

     

     

    Kara closed her eyes and exhaled heavily. "It's fine. I'm not sure anything James could do at this point will ever make me trust him again."

     

     

    "Still. I'm sorry. I guess I shouldn't have gotten carried away and helped him with Guardian. That kinda changed him a bit, I think."

     

     

    "It's not your fault, Winn. Some part of James was probably always like that and circumstances just made it easier for it to come out now. Don't beat yourself up over it."

     

     

    The two friends talked for a little longer, Lena happy to just be there and run her fingers through Kara's hair. Alex came into the room finally, and she immediately apologized for taking so long to come back.

     

     

    "If I'd known you were this hurt, I would have stayed behind," she commented, looking over the injuries that were taken care of.

     

     

    "What about Jeremiah?" Kara asked, more concerned about that than herself. When Alex just deflated and shook her head, explaining that the place was deserted when they got there, Kara looked stricken. "I'm so sorry, Alex."

     

     

    Alex shrugged. "At least we know he's alive. We'll rescue him soon. We just have to figure out what Cadmus is doing and find them, that's all."

     

     

    "Wait, Cadmus?" Lena asked, eyes going wide. "That was the name of the government alien research group Lex founded before he started his reign of terror and it got defunded and shut down. It's still active?"

     

     

    "Yeah," Alex answered, nodding. "They haven't been that active until recently, and they're escalating since I'm positive they're the ones behind your kidnapping." Alex huffed angrily. "I just don't understand what they could have wanted."

     

     

    Kara shrugged. "They took samples of hair, skin, and blood. Your guess is as good as mine."

     

     

    Alex looked at Lena. "Do you remember what Cadmus had been researching when it was government funded?"

     

     

    "Unfortunately, no. I wasn't involved, and Lex was really secretive about it, claiming government NDAs meant he couldn't talk about it," the brunette answered.

     

     

    "I guess we'll just have to be vigilant."

     

     

    Alex made Kara lay under the lamps until she at least wasn't dizzy anymore, making Lena move so she could turn them up on high. In the end, Kara was under them for almost two hours even though she claimed she was fine after an hour with no one believing her. Bags and car recovered, Alex rode along with them back to Lena's house with a bag full of DEO security devices to bulk up Lena's already substantial security system.

     

     

    "How are things with Maggie?" Kara asked when they were all finally just relaxing in one of Lena's living rooms. Lena was on her laptop, which Kara decided to ignore given everything that happened. Despite the fact that she could see Lena's email was open, most of the inbox was being ignored in favor of sending out several emails. Even if it was related to work, it seemed more like Lena was putting things in motion more than working on her current workload. So she let it pass for now in favor of talking to her sister.

     

     

    Alex sighed and shook her head. "She said she doesn't feel the same way about me, which is fine. Turns out she doesn't want kids so it's probably a good thing. We're going to stay friends though."

     

     

    "I'm sorry she doesn't return your feelings," Kara told her softly, "but at least you've gained a new friend that can help you navigate everything."

     

     

    Alex nodded, though she still looked a little sad about it. There was a level of acceptance too, of course, but Kara knew her sister was strong. She'd be okay once the initial pain of rejection passed.

     

     

    "Speaking of Maggie, she said you called in a weapons shipment tip?"

     

     

    Kara nodded. "Yeah. James asked me to go along with him to check it out." The rage on her sister's face at that news worried her. "Why? What happened?"

     

     

    "There were a couple crates with alien weaponry, but more than that was the fact that the place had kryptonite dust all over the floor."

     

     

    Lena's head shot up. "Wait. You mean James was going to lead Kara into a trap?"

     

     

    "Looks like it," Alex told her, nodding. "I'm not sure if he was aware or not, but if he was..."

     

     

    Kara stood, her face twisted with emotional pain. "I need to go lay down." Lena started to put her laptop aside, but the blonde shook her head at her and walked off. A moment later, they heard the back door open and close.

     

     

    "She'll be okay," Alex assured Lena, seeing the brunette look stricken. "Sometimes Kara needs to get away and just... think about stuff. Especially when she hurts."

     

     

    Lena nodded slowly, and after a moment she pulled the laptop back onto her lap. "I'm making arrangements to have my CFO move to National City and take on a few of my responsibilities. She's been wanting to move anyway, she just wanted to wait until next summer. But apparently her daughter has been having some issues at school so she's been asking about moving this winter instead."

     

     

    "Why are you lessening your workload? And won't she end up getting swamped?"

     

     

    Lena shook her head. "I had been a lot more hands on with the company finances than is necessary, especially with Sam at the helm of the department, so really she's had little to do. I'm essentially handing over all the work she was supposed to handle instead of doing it myself."

     

     

    They sat in silence a little longer, and then Alex cleared her throat. "So, um, Thanksgiving is this week. I'm sure Kara forgot about it with everything happening, but would you like to join us? Winn will be there, and James would have if he wasn't being an asshole right now. I think Tenar is going to be there this year, and I invited Maggie. Mom comes in and usually stays for the weekend, but I think she said that the hotels have been booked so she's not sure this year."

     

     

    "She can use my penthouse or stay here," Lena offered. "And if we need room for everyone, I can host here. None of the guests you mentioned, save James, are people I would worry about having access to my lair here."

     

     

    Alex chuckled at her use of lair. "Honestly, that would work better than everyone cramming into Kara's apartment like we usually do."

     

     

    That image made Lena wince. "While her apartment is rather spacious for a studio, that does sound like a rather tight fit."

     

    Chapter End Notes

    The comments have spoken, and Sam seems the clear winner. I wasn't quite sure about whether I could portray Maggie well anyway, and though she will still be present in the story at time it is actually a bit of a relief in the end that I don't have to make her a bit more prominent by pairing her with Alex.

    Kelly will probably still be in the story as well, and I think I have the perfect pairing for her as well if I do bring her in. As someone said, the group kind of needs her to balance their energy and I definitely agree.

    The fic is going to end up longer than I anticipated because I'm going to mess with S3 some now that I've got Sam definitely coming in. The timeline from the show will be accelerated, but I don't think y'all will mind so much. The show is basically just inspiration and a guideline at this point anyway.

    The next question is, Sam is Reign or Reign is separate?

    Chapter 15

    Chapter Notes

    Behold - their first time is upon us!

    I'm not really sure about this one, though the smut itself is probably fine.

    Enjoy?

    Alex left before dinner, which was when Lena realized that they hadn't even eaten lunch and went to make something. Once she had food in the oven, she went out back to look for Kara and frowned when she realized she couldn't see her. A quick call told her the blonde's cell was still in her bag inside so she put away her phone and, on a whim, headed for her lab.

     

    As soon as she stepped inside she heard mumbled anger and followed the noise to one of her small electronics stations to find Kara bent over something.

     

    "Everything okay?"

     

    Kara jumped, not having expected anyone, and turned with a tiny screwdriver held in front of her like a knife. Once it registered that it was Lena, she relaxed and put the screwdriver down. "No. I hate being powerless. I've had them for so long that it almost feels like being naked. And my veins are still burning a little from the kryptonite, which I ask that you not mention to Alex or she'll have me whisked back into that bad before I can even say kryptonite."

     

    "I won't say anything to Alex," Lena promised, "but I wish you'd told me earlier. Is there anything we can do?"

     

    "Not really. I spent some time outside in the natural sunlight until I got too restless and came in here." She motioned at the scraps of metal on the table and sighed. "Not that this is going quite as planned either. I know how to do what I want, but I also usually have my microscopic vision to help with these things."

     

    "I hadn't heard about that power."

     

    The blonde shrugged. "It's not needed as much as the others, and not visible to others, so we just don't advertise it. I've also got a handful that Kal doesn't because I was under the red sun longer and developed differently. I think he has a few I don't too for the reverse reason, though he rarely sits around long enough to compare notes."

     

    "Sounds like you resent that a little." Lena stepped up next to Kara and quickly continued by saying, "So what are you working on? Maybe I can help."

     

    "I'm trying to make a signal watch for you. Well, the electronics for one. I thought I'd leave you to design the watch itself maybe? I'm not sure what kind of aesthetic you'd like for it. Or maybe a pendent? I know I can make a pendent."

     

    "Why not a bracelet?"

     

    Kara grinned and looked up with a teasing look. "That's like a wedding ring on Krypton, Lena. Are you telling me you want to be married right now?"

     

    "Only if you're willing to shackle yourself to a Luthor," Lena shot back, though her tone betrayed the fear she had about it.

     

    "Hey." Kara turned to her fully and put her hands on Lena's face. "I love you, Lena. You're amazing, you know that? Just you, just Lena. Forget the Luthors. I love Lena. You're smart and brave and kind and such an good person." She put her hand on her rib right over the mark. "This led me to get to know you, but you made me fall in love with who you are."

     

    Lena felt the first tears slide down her face, two thumbs brushing them aside. "I love you too. God, it feels like we barely know each other and yet-"

     

    "-it feels like we've been together forever." Kara smiled and pressed her forehead to hers. "I feel the same way, you know?"

     

    They leaned in and embraced, Kara pressing a kiss on Lena's forehead. After several moments, the brunette sighed and slowly pulled away. "I realized when Alex left we hadn't eaten since breakfast and it's getting late so I put something in the oven. I still need to prepare a couple sides if you'd like to help?"

     

    "Sure." They cleaned up the table a little before leaving the lab, and halfway there Kara hummed. "You do realize I won't eat as much since I'm not burning calories from metabolizing sunlight?"

     

    Lena frowned for a moment before shrugging. "I suppose I'll have leftovers then. It will be a novelty given I'm planning to keep you around."

     

    Kara chuckled. "Yeah, leftovers don't usually exist with me around unless you ask me to leave some."

     

    "That is just fine with me."

    The agent looked up at her name and then shrugged when she realized what must have happened. "It's not like either of them are related to me," she commented, "and unlike some of the men, I won't be tempted to use it for material later."

     

     

    Alex nodded once towards her. "Thank you for your sacrifice, Vasquez. You are now on Supergirl comm duty as well as calling her in for missions. There's no extra benefits to the position, though, except the ability to ask Kara to run by a food joint across the globe if she's on patrol and nothing is happening."

     

     

    "That is a coveted benefit, ma'am," the agent responded before turning back to her terminal. "Totally worth leaving the desert base if I can get that kind of bonus."

     

     

    "Anyway," Alex started as she waved for Lena to follow her, "since this toxin or whatever it is only targets aliens and I'm currently the only expert on site and you are intelligent enough to follow lab procedures, I'm having you help us with getting samples and running tests on them. I'll be checking the results for anything that stands out even if I'm not entirely sure what we should be looking for. When Mom gets here we'll at least have gotten a start though, and anything we can do to speed up our search for answers is helpful right now."

     

     

    Lena and Alex worked with a couple other medical personnel in a quarantine room for several hours before Eliza walked in. She was updated on everything so far, and after looking through the results she nodded and said, "It's a virus, and some of this sequencing looks familiar... I think it's Kryptonian."

     

     

    "There was a Kryptonian pod that landed here last night," Alex started, but Eliza shook her head.

     

     

    "No, this is much too complex to be brought in a pod and manufactured in less than 24 hours. Whoever did this has been working on it for days if not longer." Eliza looked back at the paperwork and the test results. "This virus is also non-transmittable. Whatever infected them is the only way to get it."

     

     

    "That means we can leave the lab and talk to Kara about any Kryptonian viruses she might know about," Alex decided. "Decon showers just in case anyone, people."

     

     

    The showers were unpleasant, but soon enough they were in a room with Kara and telling her what they learned. As they spoke, Kara started shaking her head at the news.

     

     

    "This is like nothing I've ever even heard about on Krypton," she told them.

     

     

    "We need to figure something out, and before they find a way to make it airborne on a larger scale."

     

     

    "Fuck." They all turned to Lena at the curse. "L-Corp manufactures an analogous element that would let them do just that." She moved to a computer and started typing, entering the company mainframe and hacking her own files while looking at the coding of the virus they had so far. "I don't know what some of this means since it's in Kryptonese, but I think I have an element that could be exchanged and make the virus inert. But I need more details about it to make sure I pick the right one."

     

     

    "I'll fly to the Fortress and check our datafiles," Kara decided. Lena turned and looked hopeful, but a shake of Kara's head had her deflating. "Sorry, Lena. It's too cold to take you without proper prep and we just don't have time for it right now."

     

     

    "I understand. Maybe another day when we aren't racing against an evil organization about to poison the aliens in the city?" she asked with a coy grin.

     

     

    Kara laughed and nodded. "That works for me," she told her before turning and rushing off to fly away.

     

     

    The news that it was her father that had developed the virus to begin with was a blow to Kara's beliefs in her parents once more. She'd already started doubting her mother, but to learn even her father had done terrible things was overwhelmingly depressing. But she had an alien population in the city to save, so she shook her head and downloaded everything they had on the virus before speeding back to hand the data crystal over to Eliza.

     

     

    Lena could tell something was off when Kara came back and handed something to Eliza. The fact that it was given as the blonde said, "Please, restricted eyes only. There are... secrets about this I would prefer we pretend weren't true while we deal with this," was a major clue that something was wrong.

     

     

    "I planned to have Alex and Lena help me with the data," Eliza told her. "I'll keep everyone else working in the virus itself."

     

     

    Kara nodded. "Thank you." Then she turned to Lena and jerked her head away from everyone before walking down a hallway. Lena hesitated a moment, checking with Eliza to make sure she didn't need her to start working immediately. 

     

     

    Eliza smiled warmly at her. "She needs you right now. We can handle getting started on the data, once I get that computer boy to set up his Kryptonese translation software up for us."

     

     

    "He has a translation software?" Lena shook her head and immediately added, "Not the time. I'm going after Kara."

     

     

    "Left hallway at the T, then the first right, last door on the left," Alex told her. Lena paused and looked at her with surprise. The redhead shrugged. "She probably didn't think to wait for you given how upset she looked. And that's the room she always goes to when she learns something about her home that upsets her. If it's shut and locked, just put a hand on the scanner. It'll turn red, but it will tell me and Kara someone is trying to go in. Once I confirm it's your handprint, I'll add you to our system and Kara can add you to that hand scanner from there if she wants."

     

     

    "If she wants?"

     

     

    "Yep. It's on a system separate from everywhere else in the DEO and she has complete control over who can access it."

     

     

    That was a bit overwhelming, and slightly terrifying, but Lena just thanked her for the heads up before speedwalking down the hallways. When she reached the door, she took a deep breath and exhaled slowly before putting her hand on the scanner. It beeped and turned red, and the small speaker above it transmitted Alex's voice as she asked for identification. She put her hand on it again and it went red, but the door still opened with Kara standing in the doorway.

     

     

    "Come on in," the blonde told her, her tone sharp but volume low. As soon as they were inside and the door was shut, Kara walked over to a much more high tech terminal than anything she'd seen elsewhere and tapped a few things on a holoscreen. "You can now come here whenever you like."

     

     

    "Where exactly is here?" Lena asked, looking at the sparse room. The walls were blank, and the terminal on a small shelf plus a platform in the center were the only things keeping it was being a flat boxy room.

     

     

    Kara didn't really answer, just approached the center of the room. Her proximity to the platform triggered something, and a hologram of a woman appeared in the center.

     

     

    "Kara. It is good to see you again."

     

     

    Kara was quiet for a moment, and then Lena listened to the barely contained anger in her words as she spoke to the woman. "Why would Krypton need a bioweapon like Medusa? We were considered mediators, peacekeepers! Other planets came to us when they needed help with conflicts after too long waging war and finally desiring peace. We helped people!"

     

     

    "Not every race is peaceful, <inah>. Your was tasked with finding a way to quickly and painlessly take care of any potential invaders. Medusa was the result."

     

     

    Kara roared and released her heat vision on a wall to one side. Lena jumped at the noise, startled but not afraid. She knew that she was safe with Kara, even angry as she was. That said, she knew better than to approach her, as Kara would likely have very little control of her body with the way she felt right now and if she even bruised Lena while like this she would hate herself.

     

     

    "You tricked me and used me to capture my aunt - your twin sister! - to imprison her for wanting the populace to be warned of Krypton's pending destruction. It's no wonder she became a radical with you discounting her even as you knew the truth, helping to hide what was happening. Of course she wouldn't trust humans not to do the same thing, to use mind control to make sure they didn't destroy this planet as well! And father was willing to kill so many people with this all-but-Kryptonians deadly virus."

     

     

    It made sense, suddenly, why Kara had been so accepting of her as separate from her family. Lena was heartbroken for her, to learn all this after the people in question were gone and couldn't answer for their deeds. She wasn't sure what this hologram was, had determined it was the likeness of her mother, but it was unlikely to compare to the real thing.

     

     

    Kara sobbed and dropped to her knees in front of the hologram, and Lena stepped close so that she could kneel behind her and put her arms around her.

     

     

    "Whatever your family has done, Kara," Lena mumbled into her neck, "you are so much more than anything they could ever have hoped for."

     

     

    "I used to think they were amazing, good people, people making decisions for the good of all of us. It was bad enough to know that they purposefully hid the truth of what happened even if it was under order of the Council, but to learn that my father made this virus? That Cadmus used my blood to gain access to it so that they could weaponize it now?" Kara shook her head and slumped backwards to lean further into Lena. "Rao. How can I ever reconcile that with who I thought my parents were?"

     

     

    "Maybe you can't. But they were still your parents." Lena kissed the side of her head and embraced her more firmly. "My father was an alcoholic. He would drink, and then he'd get mean. I was normally never the subject of ire during these moments, and I rarely saw him when he was drunk anyway. But I got a low grade on something, I think English because I missed something that was right across the pond but wrong here. It ended up getting me a mark down for it. And my father was angry about it, called me into his office, and he spanked me for it. Just once, and it was nothing compared to the tongue lashing that followed. I don't remember the words, slurred as they were, but I remember it was the first time I was terrified of my father who had always been so gentle with me before then."

     

     

    Kara huffed out a weak laugh. "What a pair we make, huh."

     

     

    "I think this is just further proof we were meant for each other."

     

     

    Kara pulled away enough to face her. "You believe me then? That you're my soulmate?"

     

     

    "I'm starting to," Lena admitted. "It's hard to imagine without proof, but the more I learn about you and the more time we spend together, the more I feel like maybe there is something to it."

     

     

    Leaning forward slowly, cautiously, the blonde kissed her softly, delicately even, and Lena knew it was because she was still uneasy about her control, so she took the initiative to lean in a little harder and depend the kiss. By taking control of it, Kara could relax while still getting the intimacy she needed in this moment. It was a little heady, knowing Lena had power over the hero in this moment. But she didn't push any further than this, just letting them both take from each other the need they had of touching like this.

     

     

    "Thank you," Kara whispered a while later when they pulled back slowly. "I love you."

     

     

    "I love you, too, Kara. So, so much."

     

    Chapter End Notes

    I know I ignored the brunette for the moment, but soon. Maybe next chapter, I don't know. This story writes itself, I swear.

    I'm trying to keep up with tags but I'm really bad at knowing what to add, so if you think of something and see it's not there or there's a tag you expect but don't see or whatever, let me know.

    Chapter 18

    Chapter Notes

    If you've read any of my other fics, you know that writing action is like my one weakness. Okay, so I have many weaknesses, but action is the one I have a lot of trouble working on. It just feels uncomfortable for me to write.

    That said, I tried my best to work on it for this chapter.

    They moved to sit next to each other, Lena leaning into the blonde and both looking up at the hologram of Alura together. They had been quiet for a while, both happy to be together for a moment of peace.

     

    Eventually Kara sighed and said, "Mom, this is Lena Luthor, my soulmate."

     

    Seeing the woman turn her head towards her was a little unnerving, but Lena didn't say anything about it right now. There was a short silence, then the hologram said, "Bonding registration complete. I've sent the data to the Kryptonian mainframe that I have located at-"

     

    "That wasn't why I told you," Kara huffed, gaining her attention. "I just wanted to tell my mom I wasn't really alone anymore, that's all."

     

    "While unnecessary, to tell me, I know I would have been glad to know you met your soul's match."

     

    "Yeah. Just wish you were actually here," she mumbled. "You can go ahead and shut down for now."

     

    Alura bowed her head head and disappeared.

     

    "Are we going to talk about how it sounds like your mother just married us?" Lena asked, smirking and lifting one brow.

     

    Kara blinked and gaped at her before looking over at the platform. "Well, crap. And uh, Kryptonians don't have divorce. Not that I want to divorce you, ever, and I had totally known I was going to marry you one day but like-" She huffed and shook her head. "Well. I guess at least I don't have to take you to the Fortress of Solitude to register you as my bondmate. We can just go to like, uh... I guess we can check out all the cool gadgets and stuff Kal has secreted away there sometime. There's also the entirety of Krypton's history there, though you have access to this room and the hologram can access all of that too now. I had it connected to the mainframe at the Fortress of Solitude shortly after Alex had this room made and the hologram moved here."

     

    "That's Superman's secret hideout, right?"

     

    "Yup."

     

    "Do you have one?"

     

    Kara nodded and chewed her lip as she checked that the door was still closed up tight. She checked again for listening devices, as she did every time she came in here, before lowering her voice and saying, "It isn't really anywhere we can go together though. Even Kal would have troubled with the depths that I activated it at."

     

    "Depths... like, underwater?"

     

    "Yeah. It's not as big as his, my father didn't prepare mine to be quite so large as Kal's. Jor-El was the more grandiose between the brothers, so it doesn't surprise me just how big the home he built for his son was. My Sanctuary is still grand, don't get me wrong, but if Kal's is like you home then mine is like your penthouse. It has whatever space he determined I would would have needed and little more. I have the same knowledge banks he does as well as my father's project da-"

     

    Kara jumped to her feet - Lena nearly fell over at the suddenness - and started pacing. "The Fortress didn't have an antidote or a vaccine or whatever because the virus had been completed so it was part of the Guild's records and thus in the Fortress's records. But <ukr> likely worked on something to counter it just in case it was weaponized by someone else, it wouldn't make aense not to have that precaution."

     

    "And if he did and hadn't completed it, or decided not to submit it, then it would be in his private files and therefore in your data but not at the Fortress," Lena realized, pushing herself to her feet. "Fuck. I wish I could go... Still, you should go and check."

     

    Kara nodded and smiled at her. "Thank you. I don't go to Sanctuary often because it's exhausting to swim that deep, so I didn't even consider checking there."

     

    "Is it far?"

     

    "Not really? It is in the middle of the ocean in a deep dark trench that no known sub can go into. I can barely even see down there, to be honest, so I have to be careful or I could get lost before I run out of air."

     

    Lena hadn't even realized Kara would be holding her breath the entire time. "Then be careful? I still want to finish talking about how we're married, but this is way more important."

     

    "Unfortunately, I agree," the blonde sighed. She stepped close and planted a soft kiss on her nose. "We will definitely have that conversation, I promise. I won't be as quick getting there and back as I was getting the info from the Fortress. If you just compare the distance from here to there, the Fortress is farther, but I can fly faster than I can swim."

     

    "Just stay safe and come back to me. Even without that data, we'll figure this out. I'd rather have you here with me."

     

    They left the room and walked to the lab that Alex and Eliza were working out of right now, Kara explaining a little haltingly about how she had one more place to check that it might have its own answers. Lena was surprised that the blonde didn't explain in more detail than that, but then realized that it could be because they didn't know might be listening in when she saw Kara eying the walls and ceilings.

     

    Kara left after she finished her explanation, vague as it was, and Lena quickly stepped in to start reading through the data she had brought already that the two Danvers women had already been working on. She had made a few notations in a few places and had started making her own notes separately.

     

    The flight to the Fortress and back earlier took Kara an hour overall, so Lena started to get a little antsy around that time when the blonde hadn't returned. At the two hour mark, she had to start pacing as she considered everything she'd read. Luckily, after two and a half hours, Kara walked into the lab they were in and quickly moved to the terminal Lena was using to plug in the crystal to it and pulled up several files.

     

    "Wha-" Lena started, her dropping open as the brunette quickly took in what was on the screen first thing. "Kara. This makes it inert?"

     

    "Yes." The blonde grinned at her and nodded once. "Think you can do it?"

     

    "Darling, I know I can."

     

    A few hours later, Lena and Kara were in L-Corp's chemical storage removing any of the Isotope 454 canisters currently present to hide in Lena's lab at her house. Kara had just returned from zipping off with the last of it and Lena had just replaced the current data on her servers to match the "new" compound when there was a loud explosion, one that Lena felt some of the shockwaves of but that Kara heard.

     

    "I think Henshaw might be here," the blonde hero commented lowly.

     

    Lena chewed her lip and stepped close, putting a hand on her chest. "I wish I'd been able to make a suit for you, but there is a temporary kryptonite shield in my office safe. Your handprint will open it."

     

    "Lena-"

     

    "Please. I'll be fine and you can get there faster than they will likely get down here. And I'm going to tuck myself out of sight."

     

    "As long as you promise to stay safe."

     

    Lena smiled. "There's a defensive shield prototype on one of the shelves next door I was going to go get. You need to go though."

     

    Nodding, Kara kissed her quickly before she sped off, and Lena hurried to one of the other rooms on this floor. Before she could get it open, cursing her need for long access codes, the elevator doors blasted open and an imposing black man walked out of the hole he made. The door unlocked, and Lena quickly opened it to go inside.

     

    "Miss Luthor," the man's gravelly voice called out before she could close the door. It made her stiffen her spine as she narrowed her eyes at him and said, "And who are you? Why are you destroying L-Corp property?"

     

    One side of his mouth twisted up into a smirk. "I'm glad you asked." Before Lena could say anything more or even latch the door closed, the man had rushed her, hand clasped so tightly around her forearm that it was painful and she cried out. "My employer only said to grab the isotope, but I know she wants you too. Take me to Isotope 454 and come peacefully, or I'm not sure I can promise to deliver you to Cadmus unharmed."

     

    Lena nodded and, afraid to stall too much, she stepped out of the room to close the door behind her. "It's in the next room."

     

    Henshaw released her arm, but despite her self-defense training she wasn't sure she could safely take on the man given he was able to blast open her elevator doors. There wasn't any sign of explosives on his person, nor any visible weapons of any kind, which meant he was likely enhanced in some way. She typed in the room code as slowly as she dared, and as soon as it opened they walked to where the Isotope she and Kara had put away was resting in canisters labeled "Isotope 454."

     

    Henshaw grabbed two of the nearby transport cases and opened them, placing the canisters inside carefully. As soon as they were closed up, he handed one to her and picked up the second himself before grabbing her arm again. "Let's move."

     

    It shouldn't have taken this long for Kara to get to the safe and get the kryptonite shielding she made, and Lena worried that something else had happened. The elevator still functioned, so she was pushed onto it with Henshaw blocking the hole as he pressed the button for the lobby. It was a quick ride, L-Corp had quick elevators installed given the number of floors and employees needing to be on them. Only one went to the storage areas, and it was accessible only on the lab floors or from the lobby.

     

    They made it to the lobby, and Lena learned that there were cops on site along with Corben, who was the reason Kara hadn't made it downstairs yet. A hole from the stairwell told Lena he must have been standing guard there and caught her on the way either up or down. Considering Kara was holding up her forearms to block the kryptonite beam from his chest but wasn't looking sick from it, Lena assumed it was because she was on her way back down.

     

    Henshaw jerked her forward, and Lena stumbled with a noise that distracted Kara enough that one of the beams hit her and knocked her back before she could brace herself. She flew back until she hit the front wall, debris falling near several of the cops who moved to avoid getting hurt. Two didn't make it entirely, one getting hit around the shoulder and the other the back of the head. Three other cops moved to them, one to cover while the other two checked the injured quickly. Lena didn't get to see if they were all right, Henshaw jerking her arm and dragging her down a hall towards a different exit.

     

    "Lena!" she heard called out behind her, then an ebergy blast that had her looking back before they were out of sight of the fight. Corben was covering for Henshaw's escape, not letting Kara past him so that the blonde could rescue her. She was jerked harder, and as they went down the hall she heard Kara yell, "If she's hurt with even a bruise when I get to her, I swear on Rao's holy light that you will be unlikely to see the light of Sol again."

     

    "A lot of races see oaths and vows as binding," Kara explained as they ate dinner one night. "Tenar's people rank them and determine based on culture how strong or severe they are. If I swear on Rao, it would be the most holiest and severe of vows I can take as a Kryptonian, followed closely be one made on a deceased person of importance."

     

    "So when I swear to God, is that what you mean?"

     

    "If you were Christian, yeah. But you don't hold those beliefs as sacred like I do for Rao. Still, it would be similar, yes. Tenar swore on the Goddess to be on par with that oath because her people follow their Goddess the way mine follow, followed, Rao."

     

    "So like the oaths we make in court."

     

    "That's close enough, yes."

     

    Lena inhaled sharply at Kara's words, knowing them to be the vow they were and looked at where Henshaw had gripped her tightly on her forearm. She already knew it was going to be a bruise and that Kara would do everything in her power to fulfill that promise. She didn't know if it meant that the man would be in a government hole or one six feet under, but as she was dragged from the building and tossed into a dark van Lena wasn't sure she cared.

     

    "Drive."

     

    "But Metallo-"

     

    "He'll find his own way. We need to be away from here before Supergirl gets past him."

     

    The driver nodded and immediately pulled away, the van soon mixed in with traffic and heading through the city.

     

    "You are aware that she will find me, aren't you?" Lena asked dryly. "And seeing as I am almost certain you bruised my arm, you are likely to be dead as soon as she does."

     

    Henshaw turned to her and sneered. "I'd like to see her try. She's a parasite and I would be glad to finally be able to rid this earth of her. Her and her kind."

     

    Lena shrugged and relaxed, or at least tried to appear to. "Your risks, I suppose." She didn't recognize the driver and there wasn't anything in the van she could see that would give her any information about Cadmus. "Who is this leader of yours, anyway?"

     

    "You'll see."

     

    They drove around for over an hour, though Lena was sure that actual distance from L-Corp was much smaller. When they parked and walked a little further, Henshaw dragging her by the arm again and with a tighter grip like he wanted to make sure she bruised, like he was challenging Kara's oath, she wasn't really that surprised to learn they were near one of the docks. This one looked run down and abandoned, so it was likely one of the older ones. And when she saw the woman next to a large rocket launcher, she wouldn't be surprised to learn that the property was owned by LuthorCorp. She hadn't managed to find and transfer everything the company had owned, after all.

     

    "Mother," she drawled as she was pushed forward after Henshaw dragged her over to the woman. "I suppose I shouldn't be surprised, but I suppose I hoped you wouldn't be involved in this."

     

    "Yes, well, I had assumed you wouldn't get in bed with the enemy. Especially as literally as I assume you have."

     

    Lena shrugged. "Kara's amazing in bed, what can I say? I mean, she's also just an amazing person, so-"

     

    "I don't want to hear about your alien lover."

     

    "I wasn't particular interested in being kidnapped today either, so neither of us gets what we want. Seems about par for when we're around each other, don't you agree?" She visibly seems to consider that with a hum. "Except, well, I suppose I might get to see my wife tear your henchmen in half. That I do want right now."

     

    "Surely you haven't married the creature?" Lillian asked, her nose wrinkled in disgust.

     

    "I mean, not legally in Earth's courts," Lena conceded. She was aware that the Alura hologram basically registered them as married in whatever Kryptonian database she connected to though. She wasn't about to drop on one knee tomorrow, but only because she hadn't perfected the brelacelet design she was working on. And she wasn't sure if there was more cultural stuff required. Honestly, Kara needed to find a way to bring this database to her if she couldn't go to it. "I am bonded to her I'm every way that counts off planet, however."

     

    Henshaw had finished unloading the isotope and made sure it was loaded into the rocket launchers, and when one of them was done, Lillian turned to make sure it got put in the launcher correctly. When they weren't looking, she reached up and clicked the small button on the back of her pendent, one in the shape of Kara's House crest. The pendent itself was unobtrusive, having just added the electronics to one she owned for now. It was just luck she'd finished it when Kara had gone to check out the UFO, staying up all night to do so, and immediately began wearing it out of the house ever since. She wasn't exactly sure it worked, since she hadn't tested the frequency yet, but the small, dim light she added to the front of the pendent was on so she had to hope.

     

    Lillian reached up and took a necklace off, and Lena scoffed. "Most mothers have lockets with pictures of their kids around their necks. You have the keys to a bazooka."

     

    "It's a rocket launcher, dear," her mother corrected her snidely, walking to an open priefcase computer and flipping a couple levers before grinning and looking over at her. "Pity I won't get to see your alien toy fall dead."

     

    A thump next to her had Lena grinning back. "Oh? I suppose I forgot to mention I called for her."

     

    Lillian scowled and turned the key, and Lena and Kara both just stood and watched it fly up, curving over National City. "What, not even going to try, Supergirl? "

     

    Kara turned and shrugged. "Why? It's not going to work."

     

    "What do you-" Lillian's eyes narrowed at her daughter. "You. You switched the isotope."

     

    "I did."

     

    "And I brought the police," Kara told her as sirens started. Henshaw moved to get Lillian out of here, but the Kryptonian rushed forward to slam into him. "Don't think I didn't notice Lena's bruises, Henshaw. You're never going to see Sol again."

     

    Henshaw growled at her. "You'll never be ablento defeat me, parasite."

     

    "I'm pretty sure I beat that guy up a couple weeks ago, actually? And I don't think you should be able to mistake me for him, I'm much better looking."

     

    Roaring, Henshaw rushed her and Kara met him head on. As they fought, Lena intercepted her mother before she could get in a vehicle and leave, a dark SUV pulling up behind her and blocking the van Lillian was headed for. Alex stepped out, gun drawn, and J'onn motioned for the other agents to arrest the driver while he and Alex moved to Lillian.

     

    "You won't be able to hold me," Lillian told them, holding her head up. "I'll be out by morning."

     

    Alex shrugged. "If you hadn't used a bioweapon targeting a minority citizen of the United States, maybe. Don't you read the paper? The president passed the Alien Amnesty Act yesterday, here in NC even."

     

    Kara tossed and unconscious Henshaw next to them, and Lena immediately zeroed in on the fact that her girlfriend wasn't exactly clear of injuries when she stood next to him. "Aw, when? I was looking forward to getting to meet her!"

     

    "While you were on one of you information gathering journeys," Alex commented, sighing. "I missed it too, having had to keep my eyes on the information you did bring."

     

    "Touching," Lillian drawled, "but get to the point."

     

    "Oh, right," Alex grinned, having known what she was doing. "Anyway, anything that deals with aliens, either aliens attacking or attacking aliens, is under our jurisdiction. That means you don't go to human jail to await trial, Mrs. Luthor. I think your cell is across from a lovely Hakthor."

     

    J'onn loaded Henshaw while Kara wrinkled her nose with confusion. "Isn't that the guy that won't wear the clothes we gave him? I'm pretty sure we only have one Hakthor in lock up right now."

     

    Needless to say, as Lillian was being packed away in the SUV, Lena couldn't help but take joy in the look of horror on her mother's face.
