    Chapter 19

    Chapter Notes

    I sat on this a few days, not sure I liked the reveal and stuff. But in the end I didn't come up with something better so I just... went for it. Hopefully yall aren't disappointed.

    "Because I am curious despite being afraid to know, what do the Hakthor look like?" Lena asked as Kara flee her to the DEO while the SUV with her mother took the ground path.

     

     

    "Your mythology has lamia, correct? The half snake half women creatures?" Lena made a positive noise, and Kara nodded. "Well, Hakthor have scales on their back leading down to a long, thick tail like lamia, but they have legs that are, um... curved differently? They also have no genitalia recognizable by humans. Honestly, I don't remember how they reproduce, but they do have two genders. Women have large mammaries and men are flat chested like humans. Instead of hair, though, they have these... Um, you saw Star Wars, like the first one chronologically? With Jar Jar?"

     

     

    Lena had a look of annoyance. "Yes."

     

     

    "Well, they have two tendrils off their head like that, though not nearly so flexible. It looks almost like a cobra's hood? Sorta? But it's loose and in two instead of attached."

     

     

    Honestly that wasn't nearly as terrifying as she anticipated. "Are they dangerous?"

     

     

    "Nope. He just refuses to wear clothes in public after numerous warnings. Honestly, we're trying to get housing and a job for him in a nudist friendly area so he can be free to dress or not dress to his hearts content. It's just taking a bit longer to get the paperwork to move him because there aren't any places in the US." They landed on the balcony and Lena was gently set down. "We will probably delay it if his paperwork does come in today or tomorrow just to bully your mother, honestly. Or if we have to move him, we have plenty of prisoners that are ready to move to those cells for good behavior. They are basically small apartments and meant for the short term or lower risk prisoners."

     

     

    "And Henshaw?"

     

     

    "I did vow he would never see Sol," Kara promised, caressing Lena's arm where the bruise was darkening already. "If J'onn won't put him somewhere appropriate I will be honorbound to kill him. He only lives because the DEO has places that fit the terms of my oath."

     

     

    They walked further and met up with Eliza outside the medbay looking at the chart for their mystery woman. "Any updates?" Lena asked.

     

     

    Eliza shook her head. "No. We did get a blood sample, do if you want to take to one of your databases, Kara, we may be able to get an identity."

     

     

    "I can have the hologram check it," the blonde told her, moving to the observation window. "Any reason she isn't awake yet?"

     

     

    "Her systems were taxed by reentry. One of the pod's safety features weren't functioning properly, so she was knocked around pretty hard. And since the window has reflective properties, her body wasn't in contact with yellow sun energy until we brought her here, so her body was then inundated with those changes as well." Eliza shrugged and put the chart away. "If she were anything but Kryptonian, I wouldn't be surprised by the time she's been asleep."

     

     

    "You checked for Kryptonian markers then?"

     

     

    "I did. She checks out as Kryptonian."

     

     

    Kara nodded. "I'll take the blood sample to Alura then," she said, taking the vial when Eliza held it out to her. "Thanksgiving at Lena's tomorrow?"

     

     

    "As long as Lena is fine hosting," her adoptive mother commented, smiling warmly at the brunette who warmed at the look. "And thank you again for letting me stay at your penthouse. It is gorgeous."

     

     

    Lena beamed before turning to Kara and turning her look fond. "Well, you are family," she drawled, "given that apparently Kara and I are married according to the hologram."

     

     

    Kara groaned as Eliza gasped and turned to her. "And you didn't tell me about it? Kara Zorel Danvers."

     

     

    "Eliza, I swear, it's just because we were all working on this and there seemed to never be a good time for it. Besides, we're only registered at the Fortress! I am sure we'll have an engagement and a wedding and everything according to human custom as well as Kryptonian."

     

     

    "You better. You also need to figure out that baby making tech so I can have grandbabies. I'm not getting any younger, you know."

     

     

    "Baby making tech?" Lena asked, confused and interested. She decided to add teasingly, "I hope you aren't talking about sex toys with your mom, Kara."

     

     

    Kara sputtered. "What? No! Rao... Nonono. Um, Kryptonians were made in what were essentially artificial wombs. You give the machine genetic code and tell it what the child's role in society was, maybe told it some features you'd like to see, and it would, you know, make the baby."

     

     

    "That's fascinating. Do you think it would be possible to replicate the tech even somewhat on Earth?"

     

     

    "I mean, I can't imagine why not?"

     

     

    "Were all Kryptonians born this way?"

     

     

    "Kal was the first natural birth in millenia. It was such a scandal when it got out that his mother was pregnant. Not that we didn't have sex, but our duties to Krypton had to take precedence and most women didn't have the ability to actually carry children, especially those from higher Houses. I don't, for example, because it wasn't considered necessary for my role on Krypton. But Aunt Lara was from a middle House and they didn't really have the ability to be as selective as my own regarding what the children were able to do. They were mostly only really able to choose the child's role in society and if there were physical traits they wanted to be dominant from the parents."

     

     

    Lena was gobsmacked, her jaw dropped open until Eliza put a gentle hand on her arm. "It's a little strange to hear about at first," the motherly woman told her with a warm smile. "I was outraged when I first heard about it. Eugenics is heavily frowned upon for humans, and I struggled to accept that another people wouldn't feel the same."

     

     

    "We didn't have the resources," Kara added with a sad smile. "All of our natural water sources were either dried up or toxic, we had very little forestry, any animals that we weren't raised or that weren't natural to the very dangerous Scarlet Forest were extinct. Children were born only to fill a need that we had in society, and only higher Houses didn't have to put their names in for a lottery every year to have children. There were a couple of high Houses that didn't even have children to fill roles in society, even. My aunt Astra and her husband had been denied children every year until-" Kara sighed and shook her head. "Anyway. For my family, having a child was a privilege and not a right of the high Houses alone. My mother hadn't put their names in the lottery like the mid and low Houses, but she did wait until there was a need. Jor and Lara, my uncle and his wife, didn't actually intend to get pregnant but when they did, well. It's not like they were going to be asked to terminate given Jor was an El."

     

     

    "Is being an El different than being a high House?"

     

     

    Eliza chuckled. "If you talk to Kal? Not at all. He could tell you the history of their family as written in the archives but..."

     

     

    Kara scoffed. "Kal thinks our House is just one of thirteen High Houses. But the Els can trace back to when Krypton had a monarchy, and even after millenia of being a republic our House held more sway than the others."

     

     

    "Basically," Eliza told Lena when she realized Kara wouldn't clarify further, "Kara is Kryptonian royalty."

     

     

    Lena froze. "You're a princess?"

     

     

    "No! There hasn't been a monarch in over six thousand years!" Kara told her.

     

     

    "But you just said your House was held higher anyway!"

     

     

    "Not a princess..." the blonde sulked, deciding to duck into the medbay.

     

     

    With a sigh, Eliza shook her head and smirked at Lena. "I'm afraid Alex has made her feel extremely self conscious about it. She used to tease Kara that she was a Disney princess and someday her prince would come and solve all her problems." The blonde woman laughed. "I'm sure you can imagine how Kara felt about that."

     

     

    Given Kara's love for Disney movies, Lena wouldn't have imagined it was an insult. But then, Kara would have landed here and had to listen to people tell her she couldn't be like her cousin, that he was fine and didn't need her. After hearing that enough, Lena supposed being told that Kara did need a man to help her likely poked all her insecurities and became a pet peeve.

     

     

    Kara sat down and watched for several minutes before the woman shifted and opened her eyes, quickly grabbing Kara by the throat as she stood, holding the blonde up off the ground.

     

     

    "Hey," Kara choked out before switching to Kryptonese. <You are safe,> she told her before touching the sigil on her chest. <I offer the protection of the House of El and the promise of safety.>

     

     

    The woman loosened her hold and let Kara go once her feet were in the ground. She tilted her head and looked around, eyes narrowed. "I learned this planet's main languages. It is this one I hear spoken beyond the walls." Her eyes moved back to Kara. "You are of El? Kara then, yes? It was unknown if you and Kal-El survived." The woman looked at the door then and frowned. "Why do armed humans stand outside? You said protection and safety. Am I in danger here?"

     

     

    "Only if you are a danger to the humans here," Kara told her. "I will be forced to forsake my vow to you in favor of the vow I made to protect this planet and the people who call it home were you to be a danger to them."

     

     

    "I see." She nodded and turned back to Kara. "I am Reign. I was created to prepare this planet for settlement of what remains of Rao's children. Zor-El had a plan and made a shield around Argo City but sent you and your cousin in case it failed. It did not, but we are running out of the fuel used to keep the shield running. We are hoping for refuge here, to land Argo somewhere that we may live on this planet."

     

     

    Kara frowned and considered the issue. "I cannot promise anything, but there are places that cannot be inhabited by humans but that Kryptonians could survive. There is a cold continent called Antarctica at the southern part of the planet. No one claims in for any one human government, but there are treaties in place regarding it."

     

     

    "But it would be a safe place for Argo?"

     

     

    "Possibly, yes."

     

     

    The woman studied her intently before asking, "And when your people are here, where will your loyalties lie? With them, or with your vow to this planet's original peoples?"

     

     

    "I don't see why I would need to choose."

     

     

    The woman said nothing, just studied Kara for several seconds before she sat on the medbay bed again and looked around. "This is an odd cell."

     

     

    "It's not a cell. You weren't awake so we brought you to the medbay of this facility." Kara sat back down and relaxed into her chair. "Do you have a name?"

     

     

    "I am called Reign. I was created to be the first line to prepare a place for us."

     

     

    "How many survived?"

     

     

    "Less than twenty thousand. Over the last thirty years, we have nearly tripled our population. It is still only a fraction of the population we hope to attain."

     

     

    Kara frowned, hoping that some of the undercurrent she was getting from Reign was wrong. If Argo City was planning an invasion... Kryptonians are supposed to be a peaceful people. But then, Reign may be holding back because that's just what you do before negotiations have become official.

     

     

    "If you don't mind, we will have a couple of doctors come in to make sure you're recovering from re-entry now. And the director will probably want to talk to you as well."

     

     

    Reign watched her quietly for several uneasy moments before she nodded. "Yes, this is agreeable. If you have my pod, I have a way to establish communications with Argo as well. The Council will need to be involved in these talks with your director."

     

     

    After a wave at the door, it opened and three armed agents came in, followed by Kara's family, including Lena. "Reign. These women are my family on this planet. My adopted mother Eliza Danvers, my sister Alex, and my <shesur:zhaol> Lena Luthor."

     

     

    Until Kara had introduced Lena, Reign had seemed relatively uninterested in them and had focused more on the armed agents. But as soon as she heard soulmate, her attention zeroed in on Lena. "Your mark led you to this human?"

     

     

    "It did, yes."

     

     

    There was a slight curl of Reign's lip. "And you just accept that?"

     

     

    "Why wouldn't I?"

     

     

    "A human as your mate?"

     

     

    Kara narrowed her eyes at the brunette Kryptonian. "Is that a problem? Lena is amazing, beyond worthy of being my mate with or without the mark."

     

     

    "Why would you let yourself be tied down to such an inferior-"

     

     

    Kara reached out and grabbed Reign by the throat this time. "They are not inferior, least of all Lena."

     

     

    "Your oath-" Reign choked.

     

     

    "You above all should know that such an oath means little when my mate is being insulted. Or is the education of my people no longer including Rao's bonds? Perhaps being without his light has made my people forget about his blessings?" Kara released her and Reign coughed as they glared at each other.

     

     

    Lena stepped forward and put a hand on Kara's bicep. "Darling. She needs to be examined so that they can move her to her room. Leave her to J'onn, okay?"

     

     

    "Okay," Kara agreed, nodding and stepping back once so that her human mother and sister could do their jobs. She didn't flinch when they opened up a small case with kryptonite needles, but Reign did as she jerked away from them.

     

     

    "What the hell is that?" the woman snarled.

     

     

    "It's a mineral that weakens Sol's effects on us," Kara explained dryly. "They need only to draw a little blood, and then you will not be exposed any longer unless you try to harm anyone."

     

     

    Reign snarled but allowed Alex to draw the blood she needed, the needles put away and the effects of them lessening immediately. "And why does it not affect you?"

     

     

    Not wanting to tell her so that the information didn't become public amongst her people should they do plan to invade, Kara just shrugged and commented offhandedly, "I've had a lot of exposure to it. Even if it is always painful, you can learn to get used to it." Then she turned to Lena and asked, "Are you ready to go home? It's been a long day."

     

     

    Lena hesitated a moment, studying Kara's face to make sure she didn't want to stay, to connect with Reign as another Kryptonian. Eventually she realized that Kara wanted to step away, she nodded and smiled. "We should stop by your room here, first. You were going to run a scan, right?"

     

     

    She had been, but then Reign woke up. Brow furrowed, she nearly denied the need before she realized that Reign didn't offer a House. Her mind quickly considered the complications, and then realized that Lena likely wanted to discuss their guest in a secure location here before leaving as well. So she nodded, and they left to head to the room with the hologram after stopping in the lab for the remeains of the other vial of blood in storage.

     

     

    "I'd get more detail at the Fortress," Kara told Lena as they stepped into the room and sealed it behind them, "but this should get us enough information to know more about her."

     

     

    Lena chewed her lip as she watched the blonde approached, the hologram starting up and the blood being offered to it for a scan. "Something about her seems off."

     

     

    "Yeah," Kara agreed with a heavy sigh. "I don't want to think poorly of my people, but it has been decades. What if those who survived didn't hold onto the <girod> and the teachings of Rao?"

     

     

    Alura interrupted them by saying, "Kara. This is the blood of a World Killer. You must take caution."

     

     

    "World Killer? But they're a myth."

     

     

    "I wish that were so. Unfortunately, they are far too real."

     

     

    "She said that there was a shield added to Argo City, that they have moved through space and are headed here."

     

     

    "Your father had a plan to add a shield to part of Argo City, but we decided to send you and your cousin away in case it failed. If it succeeded, the plan was to follow you and to colonize the primitive world we found and sent you to."

     

     

    "Colonize? Mom, there are people already living here. We can't just colonize a populated planet."

     

     

    "They wouldn't have the ability to stop us. But we weren't going to take over the planet, just ask if there was a portion we could use until we were able to find a more suitable location once you and your cousin were found and we had the ability to move on. Argo City wouldn't have infinite resources, though we did have renewable food and water." Then Alura frowned and said, "Incoming transmission from space. Shall I allow the communication?"

     

     

    Kara turned to Lena, who stepped up to her side and said, "Yes."

     

     

    Alura disappeared and then reappeared, this time in different clothing and looking a little older. "Kara?"

     

     

    "Mom?" They gaped at each other for a moment, Alura's expression changing from shock to excitement. 

     

     

    "You made it. Rao, Kara, you're beautiful!"

     

     

    "Mom." Kara gaped a little more and then shook her head gently before smiling. "I didn't think I'd ever get to see you again."

     

     

    "Neither did I."

     

     

    "And father?"

     

     

    Alura shook her head, face dropping a little. "He had to activate the shield and didn't make it back inside it in time. Your aunt and uncle didn't either."

     

     

    Lena could tell the news hurt, but it wasn't all that surprising either. This was already more family than Kara had ever thought she'd get to see again. 

     

     

    "We connected with the database on Earth that your cousin has set up. It says you are bonded, that you found your soulmate?"

     

     

    The blonde straightened and grinned proudly. "I did! Mom," she motioned to Lena and as she looked over Lena saw her expression soften as they both smiled at each other, "meet Lena Luthor."

     

     

    "She's beautiful. Please, tell me about yourself, Lena."

     

     

    Lena was a little hesitant and started to haltingly talk about her achievements - her schooling, her degrees, what she had done with L-Corp - and was going to leave it at that except then Kara took over and discussed her humanitarian projects and her house, and then started on a tangent about her inventions, even those that wouldn't ever see production via the company like the kryptonite shielding. 

     

     

    Then they had to steer the conversation to something more serious.

     

     

    "Mom. The World Killer-"

     

     

    Alura frowned. "What? Kara, you shouldn't ever trust a World Killer. They're dangerous-"

     

     

    "She said she was sent by Argo, Mom."

     

     

    "What?" Alura looked confused and then stricken. "No. They wouldn't- Kara, the Council had talked about waking one, to have it sent ahead to prepare Earth for our coming. But your father... I've seen his research, I know what they can do. I fought against it and they agreed to work on a peaceful solution, so they-" She cut herself off and put a hand to her mouth as she looked at Kara in horror. "My daughter. You must not let her do anything. Subdue her, kill her if necessary. She will ruin any chance we have at cohabitation with the humans." Her eyes moved to Lena. "If Rao blessed you with a human <zhaol> then I cannot in good conscience be okay with a World Killer being there. I'll have to go to the Council immediately to bring this to their attention."

     

     

    Kara opened her mouth to argue, but the hologram disappeared and the original reappeared, asking, "Can I help you, Kara?"

     

     

    The blonde sighed and shook her head. "No. That's all." As soon as the hologram disappeared, Lena pulled her close and wrapped her arms around her. "I can't just kill her. But... I will protect you, my family, my home."

     

     

    "Maybe she's not going to go on a murder spree," Lena murmured. "We can give her a chance."

     

     

    Kara nodded a little. "I want to. But if she is a World Killer, she's going to be under someone's orders. What if they command her somehow?"

     

     

    Lena didn't have an answer for that one, knowing that if Reign was given a command then she likely would follow through - and Kara would be forced to protect her home.

     

     

     

    Chapter 20

    Chapter Notes

    After making sure Reign was in her accommodations and sound dampeners were turned on, Kara had her small team meet in one of the conference rooms so she could share what she learned about her.

     

     

    "Wait, your mother is alive?" Alex asked, more concerned with that than anything else right away. She and Eliza were sitting close, and Lena wasn't quite sure what her expression was saying. It didn't look happy, but there wasn't really any one other emotion she could pick out otherwise either.

     

     

    "Apparently," Kara answered with a sigh, rubbing her face and taking comfort from Lena. "It's a bit of a shock for me, honestly. I... Rao, I grieved her, repeatedly, and she was alive? She said she has access to the Fortress mainframe, but didn't tell me how long they've had access nor what they learned, other than about my bond with Lena."

     

     

    "What all is on that mainframe about Earth or the DEO?" J'onn asked.

     

     

    "I'd assume if Kal knows, it's been added," she answered dryly. "He thinks it's a museum or something. I've seen his room of trophies, and honestly it's a little creepy and only marginally secured. Anyone with El blood can open it just by accessing the console."

     

     

    "Like my mother did to get Medusa?" Lena asked with a wry grin that had Kara returning it sadly. "Does he not have backups in place?"

     

     

    "I mean, you'd be hard pressed to find someone strong enough to open the door and be able to brave the terrain if they can even find it," Alex pointed out with a scowl. "There's a reason Henshaw ended up in a really deep dungeon cell, reinforced with everything we could find."

     

     

    "Before we get too distracted," Eliza interjected with faint amusement, "What are these World Killers?"

     

     

    Kara slumped forward. "They were a myth my father told me. There was this sect of witches that followed the teachings of Cythonna and they found a magic that would bond to infants, making them deadly, the perfect weapon. There are many tales, each cautioning us of a different thing the World Killers are capable of. Stronger, faster, with abilities that were deadly to even the most hardy.

     

     

    "My father had apparently been experimenting with them, had found one dormant and had looked for a way to control them. When he struggled, he worked on Medusa. It's in that information I pulled from Sanctuary, though I admit until I learned what Reign was I didn't look beyond anything specific to Medusa. I had all of his entries from that time, though, so when I combed the info further waiting for this meeting I found everything he had recorded about the World Killer he had been trying to control."

     

     

    "Was he successful?" the Martian asked.

     

     

    Kara studied him and then pointedly glanced at the recording devices in the room. "Whether he was or not, it doesn't change that we have one here."

     

     

    "If he was though, we night be able to use that to keep her from attacking us," Alex pointed out.

     

     

    "I will not control anyone, regardless of the reason," the blonde snarled, her fist hitting the table hard enough her fist went through it. Her body, tense from the anger she felt at the suggestion, relaxed marginally when Lena put a hand on her fist and an arm around her shoulders after pulling Kara into her.

     

     

    "No one will let that happen," Lena cooed. Then, realizing she couldn't guarantee that, she amended to, "I won't let that happen, darling."

     

     

    Luckily Alex didn't push, and J'onn caught enough from Lena that he wouldn't either. He instead cleared his throat and said, "We should be able to subdue her with our kryptonite stores if she does end up-"

     

     

    "You still have kryptonite?" Lena interrupted. "You work with Supergirl but have something deadly to her on-site?"

     

     

    "It's too dangerous to not have something to defend ourselves if there were rogue Kryptonians," Alex pointed out, making Lena scoff. "What, you think because you're Kara's soulmate you're suddenly the expert?"

     

     

    Lena shrugged. "Have you ever asked Kara what she thinks? About how much krypronite hurts? Do you not want to find an alternative? I understand why you'd want to ignore everything my brother ever did, but you could at least take a look at what he managed to do to make Superman human for a while. He knew about kryptonite, but he was so obsessed with proving himself superior, to beating him with his own hands, he turned the sun red so that he and Superman would be on even footing." She shook her head and felt Kara's fingers lacing between hers. "You have options, so why do you want to choose the most painful and deadly of them with someone you say you love?"

     

     

    The only person who looked thoughtful instead of angry or defensive was Eliza. "She does make a good point. Lex had gone to prison before Kara exposed her existence to the world by saving that plane, and yet you've only ever worked with kryptonite even when those Kryptonians from Fort Rozz learned to make their own kryptonite shielding. Given her mindset at the time, I could understand the use of it when she was infected with RedK, but you could have tried to subdue her with the red sunlight before resorting to it."

     

     

    J'onn slumped a little. "At the time we were under the purview of the army, and they had no interest in using anything except kryptonite. Not even to find funding for research. Right now I answer directly to the president instead, so I'll put a bug in her ear about it. That's the best I can do, however."

     

     

    "It's better than you did before," Lena quipped, earning a gentle squeeze to her hand that made her sigh and lose her defensiveness. "Anyway, until we know what Reign is going to do, we could try some sort of shielding to prevent her from absorbing the yellow sun. Thanks to my work with the kryptonite shield, I think I can isolate the radiation to build one in a day or two. As a backup we can have a red sun emulator built into an ankle monitor."

     

     

    "I'll have to talk with my mother about if those are acceptable compromises given Reign is technically part of a group of incoming refugees," Kara added softly. "I know I'm biased, so I want to believe the best of my people, but even though I don't want to take any risks with her being loose, I also don't want to insult the incoming Argonians."

     

     

    "Being diplomatic isn't a bad thing," J'onn told her. 

     

     

    "Seriously? There's a woman who was created to destroy us here and you guys want to hold hands and sing kumbaya or something?" Alex snapped, standing up. "What the hell?"

     

     

    Kara stood as well. "I was created to develop a way to harness our energy core in a way that wouldn't be so intrusive and damaging, Alex. I was created to be a scientist. I'm a reporter, granted so new that I barely have my foot in the door and it only opened because Cat Grant thinks so highly of me that she let me have my pick of careers. But that's not anywhere near what I was created to do." She shook her head and helped Lena to her feet before they walked towards the door. "Unless there is some sort of mind control, I won't treat her poorly because of what she created for. And even then, I'm going to do whatever I can to help her. If that's a problem, let me know. I have other options for holding her, for protecting her, besides the DEO."

     

     

    "If she goes missing, I'll have a warrant for L-Corp and Lena's properties drawn up so fast it'll make even your head spin," her sister snarled.

     

     

    Kara stopped at the door and smirked. "Good thing I had no intention of taking her to either of those then, huh? And perhaps you should remember that technically the Fortress and Sanctuary are foreign soil with no ties to any of your human governments. Not that I'd take her either of those places either, even if you could get to Sanctuary safely to search there." She shook her head and opened the door. "Or maybe you shouldn't be such an asshole sometimes."

     

     

    The door closed behind them before Alex could say more, and Kara heard J'onn command her sister to stand down before she made things worse followed by Eliza lecturing her about family and the fact that Kara was her sister.

     

     

    "I know she's just scared and wants to protect me," Kara mumbled just loud enough for Lena to hear, "but her lack of faith in me and her knee jerk reactions hurt."

     

     

    "Thanksgiving in a few days should be interesting," Lena drawled and making Kara laugh. They took their time walking to the large balcony before Kara sighed and opened her arms to Lena in invitation. "Mine?"

     

     

    "I know Eliza has a gate code, but you haven't sent Alex one yet have you?"

     

     

    "Nope. She has to ring like anyone else and hope I'm magnanimous enough to answer," Lena told her cheerfully. "I had planned to let you know the code for Thanksgiving on the morning of for everyone you wanted to invite."

     

     

    "Good."

     

     

    Before Kara could lift up, though, she was stopped by Winn calling out to her. She turned to him and smiled warmly. "Hey, so, um... I can't get into the conference room and they're all ignoring their phones I guess? But um, Kelly Olsen is here about her brother."

     

     

    "When is Lucy going to be here?"

     

     

    "In about an hour, and I checked - no General Lane. Did you know she's recently retired from the Army and JAG? Apparently she's looking at civilian sector here in NC but wasn't arriving until today."

     

     

    Kara beamed. "That's great! I love Lucy." She turned to Lena and added, "I think you will too. You have a lot in common, actually."

     

     

    "Oh?"

     

     

    "Yeah! You're in male dominated fields and in high positions, are in the shadows of your older siblings and judged because of them, and you're short!"

     

     

    Lena was okay with every point made, as painful as they were, until that last one. "Kara. Did you just call me short?"

     

     

    Her tone must have warned the blonde to the danger because she froze before swallowing hard and questioningly answered, "Um. No?" Lena's brow rose, but before she said anything the blonde turned to Winn. "Have Miss Olsen escorted to the interview room outside low risk holding, please? I'll meet her there."

     

     

    "Sure thing."

     

     

    Lena waited until he walked away before she asked, "You aren't going to wait for Lucy?"

     

     

    "I mean, I'm not going to talk to her about James and what happened," the hero told her as she led them down the halls and into an elevator Lena hadn't known existed before, "but I will offer her food and drink from anywhere in the world and see if she'll accept my company. It is kinda my fault he's in lockup here instead of dealing with the usual human authorities."

     

     

    Lena wasn't quite so blasé about what James had done, and definitely didn't agree that it was Kara's fault even peripherally, but she let it go for now. Especially when the doors opened and Lena realized they weren't at the low risk floor. "Where are we?"

     

     

    "The Kryptonian holding area." They walked down a long hallway and Lena watched as tan skin took on a greenish hue as the blonde keyed in to enter a thick door. It opened after a long string of code, and then they walked inside to find a room with a lot of kryptonite emitters surrounding a glass cage, a small, short wall blocking a corner to allow for the smallest amount of privacy to use the bathroom. Laying on the cot inside was Reign who sneered as they walked in.

     

     

    "You promised safety," the World Killer snarled, pushing herself up and barely managing to stand with any semblance of strength.

     

     

    Kara sighed and turned off the kryptonite. "They were supposed to take you to a private quest room. I was afraid this was where they took you, and as soon as I had a moment I came to see with hope that I was wrong."

     

     

    "When I'm at full strength again, I will tear this place down and all the humans inside will be made lifeless by my own hands!"

     

     

    "Please. If you do that, it will ruin every angle of negotiation I have for the refugees on Argo."

     

     

    "Why the hell should I believe you?"

     

     

    Kara shrugged. "I don't really have a good reason, honestly. I won't ask you to just trust me, especially after this, and my track record doesn't really prove I have your safety prioritized does it? But if you can wait until I can get word from Argo again, to talk to them about options to make the humans less antagonistic while they talk with Argo, I will do everything in my power to make this up to you."

     

     

    Reign was quiet for a long while, studying first Kara and then Lena. "You. Human. What makes your race worth not being annihilated for New Krypton."

     

     

    "Honestly, humans kind of suck," Lena told her, earning a grin. "We're contrary, we fight easily and over dumb shit, and we are so divided that we look like the worst kind of people as a whole."

     

     

    "You are not selling your people well."

     

     

    Lena shrugged. "We have a lot of problems. But we're also young and stubborn. We don't have the history that Krypton had, that a lot of alien races that have made this their home have. And despite all the struggles and idiocy, there are so many people who do their best every day to make a difference. There are humans that will give everything they have to help their neighbors, that work hard every day to fix the damage we've done with our short tempers and even shorter foresight, that want to help and learn and make this world better even if just one day at a time. We may not be worth it as we are, but I think with time, patience, and teaching we could be an amazing people."

     

     

    That left Reign thinking for several moments before she nodded once and went back to laying on her cot. "I can give a chance for you, Kara Zor-El."

     

     

    "Thank you. If you need anything, let me know. I'll try to visit as often as I can."

     

     

    They left then, the ride to a different floor quiet as they both considered what they had just seen. Lena had a feeling Kara might be removing Reign based on what they saw, the way that Alex had been acting and J'onn had been placating, but she wasn't about to ask about it while they were still here. She wasn't sure how she could help, but she held Kara's hand and leaned close to provide whatever support she could right now. Her mind was already going over the ideas she had for ways to change the shielding.

     

     

    After a short walk when the elevator doors opened again, Kara seemed to perk up at the sight of a short brunette talking to a darker skinned woman outside of a room.

     

     

    "Lucy!"

     

     

    The short brunette turned and smirked. "Hey, there, K-dawg," she greeted playfully as they approached. The two hugged, and Lucy groaned like she was being squeezed too tight - and Lena realized she may have been given the exuberance Kara had shown for her presence.

     

     

    They separated after a moment, and then Lena was pulled into Kara's side. "Lucy. My bondmate, Lena."

     

     

    "Damn, girl. I heard you were dating Lena Luthor, but married? That's news."

     

     

    Kara shrugged. "It's not really a human thing binding us, so married isn't the right term. She's my <shesur:zhaol>, my soulmate, so if we were on Krypton she would be called my bondmate." She turned to Lena and offered a sheepish smile. "I guess since we talked to Alura, I just sort of leaned into my culture for explaining our relationship rather than human culture."

     

     

    "It's fine," Lena assured her, rubbing her arm with her free hand. "While I may not understand or be on the same page entirely," she continued softly, "I do understand your culture and won't condemn you for it. I won't be leaving you for it when I can see myself at that same point someday, probably soon."

     

     

    Kara beamed, and Lucy made a gagging noise. "You're way too cutesy," the woman told them before she sighed and waved to the other woman. "Anyway, this is Kelly Olsen, James's sister. Why don't we go inside and we can talk about this? I accepted a job with the DEO legal team this morning and have clearance for everything J'onn does, so we don't have to hold back here."

     

     

    Kelly cleared her throat. "And will James have representation?"

     

     

    "It depends," Lucy told her as they walked into the room and took their seats. "If he's being tried through civilian methods, which we're going to determine today, then he'll be transferred to NCPD holding and be offered legal counsel there. But if he's being tried using the laws for aliens, which is why he's currently in holding here given it was an alien related crime, then it gets a little hazy since not all the laws for those have been established and we'll be using this as a trial for several things."

     

     

    "Miss Olsen," Kara said softly, "James was one of my best friends, and he is Kal's. My goal is to see him transferred today. The problem is, this is a DEO case and I'm a contractor with them so I don't really have power to make those decisions."

     

     

    "But," Lucy interjected, "she is the victim and so her word has a lot of power over what will happen. At some point I'll go get James so we can hear his side, though all of you will have to be in a side room listening in while I talk to him. An agent will have to be present with me as well."

     

     

    "Request someone other than Alex," Kara told her quickly. When Lucy gave her a questioning look, the blonde deflated. "She's overprotective of me, and because of some other things happening right now I doubt she'd be able to keep quiet if he said something she didn't like."

     

     

    Lucy nodded. "That could cause issue with the entire case if she did." She turned back to Kelly. "I will have an NCPD detective in the room with me as well to make sure that we're being fair to him as well. Unfortunately, other than that we don't have enough civilians vetted to be able to be present."

     

     

    "Can I meet the detective? Talk to them?" Kelly asked.

     

     

    "Sure." Lucy shrugged. "Maggie Sawyer is pro-alien but I talked to her on the phone while I was on my way here. She's the DEO liaison for NCPD and takes her role as being on the side for humans here very seriously."

     

     

    "Yeah, definitely make sure Alex is far from here for that one," Kara sighed. At the questioning looks, she told them, "She came out and confessed feelings for Maggie, but Maggie doesn't like being with new baby gays because she doesn't want to feel like the experiment. Alex didn't take it well."

     

     

    "That is understandable, but I understand why it would be hurtful," Kelly commented.

     

     

    They spoke a bit longer about what happened, Kara's testimony to Lucy painting a hurt man's actions being wrong but not an anti-alien terrorist like he was being charged.

     

     

    "I think we will be transferring him," Lucy told her as they wrapped up, "but I still want to hear from him."

     

     

    "He doesn't know what Lena is to me," Kara told them softly. "I refused to explain because he was being belligerent, but maybe if I had taken the time to tell him he wouldn't have done any of this."

     

     

    Kelly sighed and shook her head. "I love my brother, Kara, but he was changed by what Lex did to him and to your cousin. I don't think Lena being anything except your enemy would have kept him from reqcting poorly. I still can't believe he would do what you said, but... Well, with a Luthor involved it's more likely than I want to believe."

     

     

    They moved to another room and Maggie, an agent, and James were led inside where Lucy waited for them. She read off the charges and James tried to divert Lucy's attention away from them, using their history, but she wasn't deterred. Maggie talked to him about it as well, but he gave them only the basics.

     

     

    "I went to Kara with the information so she could help," he told them, not disclosing thst he would have been going as well. "She's the one that said to call the police and ended up making the call. It's not my fault she didn't go and check it out first."

     

     

    "It's not her job," Maggie pointed out. "Supergirl is to help with natural disasters and alien threats. It's part of the contract she has with the government. We ask for help in situations we could use her abilities to make it easier to protect lives, but even when we include her on stings she's only involved for reconnaissance and defense for a reason."

     

     

    James scoffed. "If she did more, was more like Superman-"

     

     

    "God, James," Lucy interrupted, "so what? Not everyone has to compare to him and be exactly like him. Kara is allowed to be her own person and to work with us instead of apart from us."

     

     

    James sneered. "And if she was, she wouldn't be brainwashed by a Luthor right now."

     

     

    "She isn't brainwashed. Lena Luthor is her person, James. It's some cultural thing and I don't have all the details yet, but I know enough that there's no way that woman doesn't adore the shit out of Kara just as much as Kara does her." Lucy sighed and shifted her paperwork into a stack and slid it into a folder to put away. "You're lucky she holds your history together high enough that she's not pressing charges, Mr. Olsen."

     

     

    "Why would she get to press charges?"

     

     

    "You tried to lure her into a trap based on some sketchy info from a dude that specifically mentioned he was going to kidnap Lena Lutbor."

     

     

    "Her mom needed to talk to her and she wasn't answering calls!"

     

     

    "Not everyone has a good relationship with their parents. You know I don't and even encouraged me to not answer when my dad called, so why shouldn't Lena get the same option?" He didn't have an answer for that. "Besides that, they gave you that address right?"

     

     

    "Yes," he sulked.

     

     

    "Do you know what the cops found there when they looked into the call from Supergirl?"

     

     

    He shrugged. "They said they were going to rough her up a little."

     

     

    Kelly inhaled sharply next to Kara and put a hand on her arm. "I really didn't think he would have known." Kara gave her hand a pat but didn't say anything, just offering silent support.

     

     

    "Have you ever asked her what it feels like to be exposed to kryptonite?" Lucy was asking. "She told me once, after my father injected liquid kryptonite into her aunt."

     

     

    "It hurts. Superman told me about it once, said it was like getting stabbed with needles."

     

     

    "Superman doesn't feel it like Kara does. She spent a lot longer under Krypton's sun, James, and so her body is a little more sensitive to different radiation levels. I won't go into detail, because that's up to Kara, but it does more than just feel like needles."

     

     

    Lena gave Kara's hand a squeeze to remind her she was there when she realized the blonde was shaking a little, tears gathered in her eyes. "Will you tell me, later?" She knew it hurt, had seen the effects and witnessed how much agony Kara was in, but also knew that her girlfriend would never tell anyone exactly how it felt - even Lucy. She wondered if the short brunette even knew about it entirely or just saw how Kara reacted and bluffed that Kara told her.

     

     

    But Lena saw the slight nod and left it at that, looking at Kelly to find her holding Kara's arm with silent tears on her face.

     

     

    James was escorted back out after that, and Lucy and Maggie stayed behind while Kara escorted everyone back into the room. Kelly was introduced to the detective, and they all sat there in silence for a long minute.

     

     

    "I don't think he'd get a sentencing in human courts," Maggie admitted with a sigh. "I mean, he'd get fined or something. He did eventually call in your kidnapping even if he did witness the whole thing and was involved with the kidnappers. A good lawyer would spin that as him having had a change of heart and making a call once he was sure he would be safe."

     

     

    "I agree," Lucy told them and shrugging. "Since you don't want to press charges either it's not likely it would even come up. And a decent lawyer would tell a jury that he was drunk and probably didn't understand the threat against Lena anyway, and the warehouse was a legit concern because maybe he thought that was where Lena would be taken."

     

     

    Kara sighed and let herself lean into Lena, who put an arm around her. "It's fine. I mean, Alex will probably raise a fit, but I don't really want this to cause problems for him. It's not like I'll ever trust him again anyway, so he's never going to be able to repeat what happened."

     

     

    "I'll suggest he go to visit our aunt for a while," Kelly chimed in quietly. "I was moving here for work so I could be near him, and given my new job I won't be able to go along, but it would out space between the two of you. Our aunt lives in a small town in Southern Oregon. If he needs to be monitored or whatever as well, that's fine." She looked up and met Kara's eyes. "My brother is a good man. I don't know what made him capable of doing this, but he isn't a bad person."

     

     

    "I agree," Kara told her, nodding. "I won't make him leave either, but if you think a visit to your aunt's is a good idea I'll try to make sure he gets to return to a job at CatCo if he wants it. Cat Grant doesn't exactly listen to me, but if I ask her for something like that she probably won't ignore it. Being a main source of news revenue does have perks," she added jokingly.

     

     

    "Thank you, Kara," Kelly told her. "As long as working with him won't he a problem for you."

     

     

    "If he ends up being a problem in the workplace, even I won't be able to save his job from Cat's wrath. And she will be warned about the basics of what happened, if you want to make sure he's aware of that. I left a note for his absences so that these last several days won't count against him, but Cat probably knows something is up. I don't want to ruin his career, so it's up to him how he handles things going forward."

     

     

    "You're a lot more forgiving than most."

     

     

    Kara shrugged. "He's done a lot for me and my cousin. But this is as far as that will take him with me."

     

     

    "Will Kal try to make you change your mind?" Lena asked, her tone betraying her unease regarding the Man of Steel. She doesn't have anything against him, and doesn't blame his suspicions against her, but he hasn't endeared her to him either. And since he has very few weaknesses, if he decided to come after her she had little she could do to defend herself. 

     

     

    "Kal isn't the Head of our House, no matter how he acts," Kara told her gently. "He can complain to me, but as long as he wears our sigil, he claims our House and as the eldest the Head of House falls on me."

     

     

    Kelly looked confused but didn't ask, and when Kara didn't elaborate Lena chose not to ask anything further about it. Knowing that Kara had familial power over her cousin was a relief as well. He had been polite to her when she saw him last, had seemed to understand her place in Kara's life well enough, but he still made her uneasy thanks to their previous interactions. She's not dumb, after all. She's fairly confident she knows his civilian identity, and Clark Kent has always been very passive-aggressive with her ever since Lex started his war.

     

     

    They went to the elevator together and Kelly got off at the lobby floor, stopping only when Kara called out to her. "You're welcome to join us for drinks sometime if you want some familiar faces to hang out with," she told the woman, handing Kelly a small slip of paper. "That's the address for Al's and my number. It's an alien bar, but they're really welcoming of everyone."

     

     

    Kelly took the paper and looked at it for a second before smiling warmly and nodding. "Thank you. I may take you up on that sometime once I'm settled in a bit more."

     

     

    "The invitation is always open."

     

     

    They rode the elevator further, getting off and walking to a familiar space. Winn saw them and waved as they walked through, heading for the balcony.

     

     

    "Alex isn't here, so it's a good time to escape," Kara mumbled.

     

     

    "We could have walked out with Kelly and I'd have called my driver."

     

     

    "Oh." Kara grinned at her and chuckled. "Yeah, that didn't even occur to me." Lena smiled and shook her head fondly, letting the blonde lift her into her arms before floating up and away.

     

    Chapter End Notes

    This wasn't exactly my best work and I know it, but I wanted to finish closing out the James stuff. I'm still not sure if he will "redeem" himself or not, but honestly I'm thinking he'll just disappear into the ether at this point.

    Thanksgiving next, and I don't know exactly how that will work out just yet.

    Chapter 21

    "What do you mean you're almost here?" Lena asked, her phone at her ear as she walked into the kitchen Thanksgiving morning. Eliza was already there putting the turkey together so it can get in the oven soon. "What about the rest of the semester?"

     

     

    "Ruby had an altercation with another girl at school, though it turned out to be instigated and started by the other girl, but when I asked if she would like me to call and ask about transferring early and spending the holiday with you she was all for it," Sam told her, a young voice in the background hollering her agreement. "I assume you're at the house and not the fancy penthouse? I think you told me your girlfriend's family was up and you offered to host, so I figured house."

     

     

    "Yes, and I'll send the house code to you as soon as we get off the phone."

     

     

    "I mean, we just pulled onto your street so I guess we can end here and I'll see you soon."

     

     

    Lena agreed and they ended their call. She typed something into her phone for a moment, and then sighed as she put the device on the counter.

     

     

    "I take it we have more guests arriving?" Eliza asked with a coy grin. "

     

     

    "Yes, my best friend and her daughter," Lena told her, mind on rooms and not on the woman currently working her kitchen. "Do I need to go get more groceries? I mean, I could send Kara to get them but then she'd come home with more desserts. I know she can eat all she wants, but why does she always get the stuff that is way too tempting so I can't say no when she offers it and then I end up eating too much?"

     

     

    "Sounds like you need to establish some boundaries."

     

     

    "But it's her culture."

     

     

    Eliza hummed and finally was able to put the turkey in the oven. "I didn't know that. Is it a courtship thing? She would always throw a fit whenever we tried to ask for any foods she had already taken, even when we got takeout. It led to an understanding that we had to take what we thought we would eat and then leave the rest for her. She wouldn't even touch our leftovers from our plates, which we never understood. She's basically a food vacuum with everything else."

     

     

    Lena thought about it a moment before deciding that Kara wouldn't mind if Eliza knew. She'd already had it explained before to Alex, though that was mostly due to Tenar. "It's a bondmate thing more than courtship. Sharing food, especially favorites, is a way to show love and affection. If I cook a meal for the both of us she doesn't necessarily share anything but if we go to a restaurant she does. We always eat different entrees because of that, actually." She chuckled and started peeling potatoes. "Of course, she usually gets three or four entrees while I have one, but I've taken to offering her parts of my plates in public as well."

     

     

    "It's interesting. I wonder why she didn't share it before."

     

     

    "Probably because it was hard. She said you always told her to hide her heritage, so maybe she thought she cpuldnt share it even with you."

     

     

    Eliza looked downtrodden by the idea. "That was never our intent. We did try to ask about things early on, usually when we had holidays we'd try to get any information on whether she used to have anything similar."

     

     

    That was an interesting question, and Lena knew that it would be easy to get an answer. Unfortunately, her phone pinged and the code she'd just sent to Sam was shown as having been used so she didn't call for her mate to ask right now. Maybe later she'd have a moment alone with the blonde to ask.

     

     

    "Sam and her daughter Ruby are here," she commented as she turned her attention back to the potatoes.

     

     

    "How old is Ruby?"

     

     

    Lena chuckled. "I can never quite remember. Twelve or thirteen maybe? She was a couple years old when I met Sam at LuthorCorp in Metropolis. I was so glad they weren't in the city the day Lex turned the sun red. I'd been working with the FBI for weeks and we were pretty sire he was getting ready to make a move based on some things I found, so I begged her to take Ruby on an extended vacation on my dime. I paid for airfare and lodging, but she wouldn't let me pay for anything else. It was until almost a week later I would let her come back."

     

     

    The front door opened and there was scuffling before it closed again. Heavy bags were dropped near the stairs, and then a tall brunette and a nearly identical teen walking behind her entered the kitchen.

     

     

    "Hey, you never let me get a convince you to get a cook before," Sam said, petulant.

     

     

    Lena flushed and cleared her throat. "Um, this is Eliza Danvers, Kara's adopted mother."

     

     

    That stopped Sam short as Ruby giggled at her mother's faux pas. "I'm sorry, Mrs. Danvers. I swear I'm housetrained."

     

     

    Eliza laughed. "It's all right, dear. Honestly, if I didn't enjoy cooking I'd have probably asked Lena to hire a cook as well," she joked. "My daughter and some of the girls' friends will be here in a few hours. I'm not sure where Kara is, though."

     

     

    Lena hummed and paused her peeling. "I suppose it would be good for you to meet her, and for her to be aware you're here." She moved to the sink and washed her hands before going to the back door and calling, "Kara!"

     

     

    A moment later, the lab door opened and a blonde blur appeared in front of her. "Yes, my love?"

     

     

    "We have a couple unexpected guests."

     

     

    "Good or bad ones?"

     

     

    Lena smirked. "That's a dangerous question, darling. It depends on the day with Sam."

     

     

    Kara brightened. "Sam and Ruby are here? I thought you said not until Christmas." She could barely contain herself, wiggling in place.

     

     

    "Oh m'gosh," Ruby behind her. "Isn't that Supergirl?"

     

     

    Sam scoffed. "Lena might be awesome enough to have a superhero girlfriend, kiddo, but I doubt she's actually dating Supergirl."

     

     

    Lena moved aside and cleared her throat with a bit of heat on her cheeks. "Actually, we're technically married?"

     

     

    That got Sam to look over finally with shock that only got worse when she got a good look at Kara. "Fuck. That is Supergirl."

     

     

    "Language," called Ruby and Eliza. Then Ruby added, "Stop embarrassing me, would you? Especially in front of Supergirl!"

     

     

    Kara laughed and walked inside so she could close the door. "I'm not in my suit so you don't need to call me Supergirl. In fact, I insist you call me Kara. Lena says you're her family, so even in the cape you could call me Kara."

     

     

    "Aw, Lena, thanks!" Sam cooed.

     

     

    "I was referring to Ruby," Lena drawled, deadpan, making Ruby laugh and Sam to look wounded, though it was obvious that she was exaggerating. 

     

     

    Kara took over peeling potatoes then, though Eliza warned her to be careful since Lena only had one peeler. There were plenty of knives, but they were very nice and Eliza didn't want to have Kara damage any of them if she got distracted and hit her hand and not the potato. Kara sulked, muttering about how Lena trusted her with her knives, but the brunette pretended she didn't hear and started helping with other food prep.

     

     

    Soon everything was prepped or cooking that needed to be done that morning, and then Lena was leading her best friend and her goddaughter upstairs to their rooms. "Since we weren't expecting you, it's not had a deep clean or fresh sheets. I'll get some for you, but for now we can at least get your things dropped off."

     

     

    "Lena, we don't need you to go through any extra effort. I know this was pretty last minute for you," Sam told her, dropping her silly act from earlier entirely. "You always take really good care of us."

     

     

    "Yes, well, I don't have many people who haven't abandoned me because of my brother, so..." Lena shrugged and motioned to the two rooms.
